iii) With regard to the domain of conducts capable of violating public peace and tranquillity prevent the emergence of criminal associations, public attitudes of instigation to the commission of crimes, participation in public riots, behaviours that prevent the exercise of political rights by citizens, disobedience to the lawful order of dispersal, criminalise practices embodying influence peddling and punish the abuse of public signs or uniforms;
jjj) Within the scope of internal security of the State provide for incriminating norms for behaviours capable of putting it at risk in a more significant way, namely enshrining the legal type of crime of treason to the Homeland, collaboration with armed forces enemies of the Timorese State, sabotage against national defence and campaign against the effort for peace in Timor-Leste, attack against the Head of State or other actions against constitutional organs or conducts that pursue as an end the alteration of the Rule of Law, diplomatic infidelity, violation of State secrecy and outrage to Timorese national symbols;
kkk) Criminalise fraudulent behaviours in electoral activities regardless of who the agent is and the phase of the electoral process, cases of obstruction to the exercise of candidacy or its unlawful exercise, situations of electoral propaganda in violation of the respective legal framework and actions that disturb the normal course of the electoral act;
lll) Life in modern societies develops in constant contact with conducts potentially generating dangers more or less close to materialisation and capable of violating interests whose integrity it is important to safeguard, such as the activity of driving motor vehicles, use and possession of weapons and certain activities with relevance in the domain of citizen and public health, and some types of crimes in this matter should be provided for;
mmm) In the same way behaviours capable of violating religious values implanted in Timorese society should be criminalised, failure to comply with maintenance obligations and others in the domain of custody of minors, cases of social solidarity arising from situations of calamity, disaster or mandatory for certain professional activities;
nnn) Criminally sanction conducts that are unsustainably harmful to the environment, namely fauna, flora, natural habitats and resources of the Timorese subsoil;
ooo) Criminally sanction conducts that attack the exercise of public authority, namely by means of acts of disobedience, obstruction, destruction, resistance or usurpation of public offices;
ppp) With regard to the exercise of the executing action of penalties or measures depriving liberty criminalise behaviours that translate into removal of prisoners, escape, assistance thereto by official and mutiny of prisoners;
qqq) As a form of protection of patrimonial legal interests enshrine two chapters grouping crimes against property in one and crimes against patrimony in general in another;
rrr) Construct a normative system protecting property based on the legal types of crime most common in various criminal legislations such as theft, robbery, breach of trust and damage, structuring these types into simple and aggravated, considering the circumstances of value combined with the nature of the thing appropriated, the means and forms of action, violence, threats or coercion or any others capable of significantly increasing culpability or unlawfulness;
sss) Distinguish, still with regard to property, the norms typifying theft of use of vehicles, cases of violence after subtraction, usurpation of immovable property and alteration of landmarks, maintaining the incrimination of involuntary damage and defining the types of prohibited burning and fire as types of crime specifically of special damage by reason of the means and of danger in view of the consequences normally associated therewith;
ttt) Protect patrimony in general not only with the most usual incriminations in this matter such as fraud, extortion, receiving and bankruptcy or insolvency but, above all, bearing in mind the needs arising from new technologies in commerce and the responsibilities to be required of those who, managing goods or patrimony of others do not conduct themselves adequately in that profession of administration, we refer to the crimes of computer fraud and the forms of harmful and abusive administration;
uuu) In modern and democratic societies justice and its exercise are interests justifying the intervention of criminal protection, and punishment should be enshrined for falsity of procedural acts, forms of obstruction of jurisdictional activity, its non-exercise as denial of justice and violation of the respective secrecy;
vvv) Include incriminations relating to acts of bribery, prevarication of magistrate or official and of lawyer or public defender, as well as other activities of personal favouritism in the domain of justice, without forgetting the classic incriminations of slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and non-reporting;
www) Beyond the references contained in the last two numbers, conducts should be criminalised that in the exercise of public functions translate into situations of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or public force or economic participation in business by those who exercise public offices or functions;
xxx) In an autonomous title, prevent through adequate incriminating norms, conducts of falsification of documents, technical notations, currency and sealed or stamped values, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals, diversifying the respective punishments according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of the falsified objects and provide for the possibility of seizure and loss of objects intended for the commission of these crimes;
yyy) Still in the same domain of the economy, incrimination should proceed of acts that constitute tax fraud or situations of smuggling and embezzlement with regard to customs or border matters, in addition to maintaining criminal punishment of disobedience to the requisition of goods ordered by the government and behaviours capable of disturbing, harming or preventing the realisation of some public acts such as public tender or judicial auction.

###### Article 3

Coordination with the Code of Criminal Procedure

The entry into force of the Penal Code referred to in this legislative authorisation law must be coordinated with the Code of Criminal Procedure and other regulatory legislation on criminal matters that is in force, as required by the legal certainty and coherence of the respective Legal Order.

###### Article 4 Duration and scope

The legislative authorisation granted by this law has a duration of 180 days, counted from the date of its entry into force.

###### Article 5 Entry into force

This legal instrument enters into force on the day immediately following its publication.

Approved on 23 September 2008

The President of the National Parliament in office, Maria da Paixão de Jesus da Costa

Promulgated on 7 October 2008. Let it be published.

The President of the Republic, Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 6/2009, of 15 July
(First amendment to the Penal Code approved by Decree-Law No. 19/2009, of 8 April)

Life, from the moment of conception of the human being, must be protected. Taking this principle as established, it cannot be ignored that there are situations that may justify the termination of pregnancy. However, the gravity of the interests in conflict here, that is, the interest (life of the pregnant woman) and the interest (life of the foetus or embryo), both with the same value, only legitimises termination in extreme situations: when the sacrifice of one life is the only and last resort to save another and there is no way to maintain both.
It is therefore important to establish that the termination of pregnancy, the final word on which must always rest with the mother, who will decide in conscience, should only be permitted when it is the only means to prevent the death of the pregnant woman.
Thus, the National Parliament decrees, pursuant to article 92, paragraph 1 of article 95 and article 98 of the Constitution of the Republic, to have force of law, the following:

###### Article 1 Amendment to the Penal Code

Article 141 of the Penal Code approved by article 1 of Decree-Law No. 19/2009, of 8 April, annexed thereto, shall have the following wording:

"Article 141 (...)

1.	(...)
2.	(...)
3.	(...)
4.	The provisions of the preceding paragraphs do not apply when the termination of pregnancy constitutes, in accordance with the state of medical knowledge and experience, and after

all possible actions have been carried out to save the life of the pregnant woman and that of the foetus or embryo, the only means to remove danger of death to the pregnant woman, provided that it is performed, upon medical certification, by a doctor or other health professional under his or her direction in a public or officially recognised health establishment, and with the consent of the pregnant woman.
5.	The verification of the circumstances referred to in the preceding paragraph is certified in a medical certificate written and signed by a panel of three doctors, on a date prior to that of the termination, of which the doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the termination of pregnancy is not a member.
6.	Consent is given in writing in a document signed by the pregnant woman – after hearing, if possible, the spouse or the person who lives with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses – or by another person on her behalf, whenever possible at least two days in advance of the date of termination.
7.	In the case where the pregnant woman is a minor, consent is given by the legal representative.
8.	In the case where the pregnant woman who is of age or emancipated is or becomes psychologically incapable, consent is given, respectively and successively, by the spouse or the person who lives with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses, by the legal representative, by an ascendant or descendant, or, in their absence, by any relatives in the collateral line.
9.	When it is not possible to obtain the medical certificate referred to in paragraph 5, and/or it is not possible to obtain consent under the terms of paragraphs 6 to 8, and the performance of the termination is urgent and cannot be postponed, the doctor decides in conscience in view of the situation, resorting, whenever possible, to the opinion of other doctors.
10.	The panel referred to in paragraph 5 is composed of doctors possessing adequate knowledge for the assessment of the circumstances that justify the termination of pregnancy, including, whenever possible, a gynaecologist/obstetrician.
11.	The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the

termination of pregnancy must provide all necessary clarifications to the pregnant woman and, if applicable, to the persons referred to in paragraphs 7 and 8, namely the method of termination to be used, the effects of the termination, the possible consequences for the physical and psychological health of the pregnant woman.
12.	Doctors and other health professionals are recognised as having the right to conscientious objection with respect to any acts relating to the termination of pregnancy.
13.	Doctors and other health professionals who invoke conscientious objection must ensure immediate intervention in the necessary acts, as well as the immediate care of the pregnant woman, by another health professional.
14.	Conscientious objection is expressed and communicated, through a written and signed document, to the clinical head of the health establishment where the objector provides service.

###### Article 2 Entry into force

This law enters into force on the day following its publication. Approved on 26 May 2009. The President of the National Parliament, Fernando La Sama de Araújo

Promulgated on 3 July 2009. Publish it. The President of the Republic, Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 13/2008, on 13 October (Legislative authorisation in penal matters)

Law 6/2009, on 15 July (Amends article 41 of the Penal Code)

Decree-Law 19/2009, on 8 April (Approves the Penal Code)

To establish a legal system, those politically responsible placed the elaboration and approval of Timor-Leste's Penal Code among their legislative priorities, in order to guarantee the fundamental rights and freedoms enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste. This legal instrument results from work that a commission, with Timorese and international technicians, developed following governmental guidance and within the limits and content established in the law concerning legislative authorisation that the National Parliament had already approved. The normative solutions adopted herein respect the social and cultural reality of the Timorese community and also accept suggestions that came from national and international organisations, contributions that came from various judicial operators working in Timor-Leste, and also teachings drawn from comparative law. We must view this Code that has just been approved, not as a final point, but as a fundamental stage in building the Timorese legal order, remaining open to future improvements that, over time, the evolution of international law, judicial practice and the teaching of law may counsel. Thus,

Pursuant to the legislative authorisation received by article 1, no. 2 of Law 13/2009, on 13 October, and pursuant to article 96 of the Constitution, the Government decrees, to have the force of law:

Article 1
Approval of the Penal Code

The Penal Code, which is annexed to and forms part of this Decree-Law, is hereby approved. Article 2 Revocatory norm

1. The Indonesian Penal Code that came into being in the legal order pursuant to article 1 of Law 10/2003 is hereby revoked. 2. Legal provisions contained in separate legislation are hereby revoked when
a) They foresee and punish acts criminalised in the Penal Code that has just been approved; b) They enshrine solutions contrary to solutions adopted in the general part of this Penal Code. Article 3 Entry into force

This instrument and this Code enter into force on the day following their publication. Approved in the Council of Ministers on 18 March 2009 Prime Minister
(Kai Rala Xanana Gusmão)

Minister of Justice (Lúcia M. B. F. Lobato)

Promulgated on 30/03/2009 Publish it. President of the Republic (José Ramos Horta)

Annex

PENAL CODE

I. The restoration of the independence of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and the approval of its Constitution, in 2002, gave rise to the need for this country to adopt its own modern legal order, which enshrines the fundamental rights contained in constitutional precepts and reflects the country's social reality. We left the Indonesian Penal Code in force because a State must have its own penal law, but that code is not adequate to the country's new reality and, often, presents legal solutions contrary to constitutional principles that have just come into being. Because the Timorese people have their own specificity and identity, their Penal Code must follow their own philosophy together with the principles and values existing in modern societies, in order to respond to the demands that this country faces. A commission, with Timorese and international technicians, which the First Constitutional Government established, elaborated a preliminary draft of a Penal Code; but, although the Law for Legislative Authorisation concerning penal law had already been approved, that preliminary draft did not receive promulgation until the end of the legislature. When 2008 began, with another executive, a new bill for legislative authorisation to approve the Penal Code entered the National Parliament, work to revise the preliminary draft of this Penal Code resumed, and this instrument underwent alterations and entered public discussion. Having received legislative authorisation, the Council of Ministers of the Fourth Constitutional Government was able to approve this Penal Code. II. Book I of the Penal Code constitutes its general part, which gathers the penal principles enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and in the conventions, treaties and international agreements that the Timorese legal order accepts. Because this code is based on the rule of law and democracy, its general part enshrines principles concerning human dignity, respect for each citizen's freedom and the State's responsibility to intervene only in situations where there is unsustainable injury to legal interests fundamental to life in society, with its right to punish the agent and its social obligation to reintegrate that agent into society. In a State under the rule of law there must also exist the principle of legality, the principle of fault and the principle of humanity. The enshrinement of the principle of legality as one of the fundamental principles of Penal Law, provided for in article 31 of the Constitution, determines that an action or omission can be considered a crime and, as a crime, give rise to punishment only in situations that the law foresees. To follow this principle, analogy cannot be applied to qualify any conduct as a crime and the court cannot use analogical interpretation to interpret norms of the Penal Code to qualify any act as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine a penalty or security measure. The principle of non-retroactivity in penal law, which prevents retroactive application of penal law, except when it favours the accused, is also a corollary of the principle of legality. The principle of humanity, enshrined in articles 29 and 32 of the Constitution, indicates the path not to apply the death penalty and also penalties or security measures of perpetual character or of unlimited or indefinite duration. The principle of necessity, the principle of proportionality and the principle of adequacy also indicate the path for the normative options that this Code follows and serve as the basis for applying each penalty and security measure, and each penalty and security measure shall be applied to protect legal interests essential to life in society and reinsert the agent into society. The principle of fault, as a presupposition for applying penalty, determines that there can be no penalty without fault (nulla poena sine culpa), and serves as a limitation on State power, because, thus, the measure of penalty cannot be heavier than the measure of fault. The principle of fault also indicates the path for the regime of error concerning unlawfulness, the path to enshrine non-imputability due to age and due to psychic anomaly, which removes the agent's criminal liability because of their lack of fault. Concerning the legal consequences of punishable acts, we see that penalties must be executed with a pedagogical and resocialising perspective, and this code has many non-institutional means to apply sanctioning measures. When there is an alternative to apply a penalty privative or non-privative of liberty, the court must give reasoned preference to the penalty that does not deprive liberty when that penalty adequately and sufficiently achieves the purpose of punishment and satisfies the need to make reprobation and prevention of crime (article 62). Thus, preference shall be given to applying alternative penalties, especially in situations concerning petty and medium criminality, with strong relevance to fine penalties and community service penalties, which are enshrined as the best means to ensure the offender's social reinsertion. Fine penalties shall be fixed by day, in order, thus, to be able to adapt to the agent's fault and economic condition, with variation in the value fixed for each day of fine according to the convicted person's economic and financial situation and their personal charges. To differentiate fine penalties as principal penalties from fines that substitute imprisonment, specific rules are also established here to regulate the conversion of fines to imprisonment in situations where the agent does not pay the fine. Concerning community service, as a non-institutional sanctioning measure, this Code seeks only to clarify and systematise some fundamental aspects of its regime and leaves its development and application in each case to autonomous law. Imprisonment must be applied only when it is seen that other penalties are not adequate to achieve the objectives of prevention and reprobation. The minimum duration of imprisonment is established here at 30 days and its maximum duration at 25 years, with the possibility of raising that maximum limit to 30 years in cases that the law especially foresees. As a consequence that comes from the offender's social reinsertion, suspension of execution of imprisonment can be applied in cases where the measure of concrete penalty does not exceed 3 years and the need to make prevention in the future does not close the path to that suspension. Suspension of execution of imprisonment can also be applied with the condition that the convicted person fulfil obligations or rules of certain conduct or with the obligation to enter into service for reinsertion supervision. This Code establishes for those non-imputable due to psychic anomaly security measures, with limited temporal duration, namely, internment measures, when their dangerousness justifies it. Measures of perpetual character cannot be applied, and this measure must cease when the dangerousness that justified that application has ended, and, for foreigners, expulsion from the country can be applied in place of this security measure. In defence of the values and legal interests that are fundamental to living in society, this Code distinguishes crimes that are public in nature, which the State must protect directly, from those that, because they are not so serious, the person who has the right to present a complaint must first exercise that right, as the penal procedural law establishes. Thus crimes shall be considered semi-public that, in the special part of the Penal Code, according to the description in their legal types, give rise to the obligation to exercise complaint. Concerning the extinction of criminal liability and the effects of that extinction, the general part of this Code fixes time limits for the prescription of criminal proceedings, penalties, security measures and accessory penalties and also defines situations that give rise to suspension of that time limit. But it establishes imprescriptibility for criminal proceedings and penalties concerning war crimes, crimes against peace and crimes against humanity and freedom. Finally, it also regulates other situations that give rise to extinction of criminal liability, such as the agent's death, amnesty and pardon. III. We know that the special parts of Penal Codes are what cause the greatest impact on public opinion, because it is in that part that the goods, interests and values that in each society and at each historical moment can receive protection from penal law and, therefore, can enter into the category of penal-legal interests are chosen. In this Penal Code of Timor-Leste the legislator has incorporated into the normative solutions that it adopts options that the Constitution has already enshrined as the sentiment of Timorese society. The systematisation adopted in this part arises from this country's history and reflects the interests and values that built this young nation. That is why title I of this book protects, respectively, peace, humanity and freedom as primordial values of democratic societies, with the respect that must be given as values that the fundamental law holds in its hierarchy and also following the international obligations that this Country has already assumed when it subscribed to and ratified the Statute of the International Criminal Court. Title II enshrines crimes against persons and protects especially legal interests that are eminently personal, such as physical integrity, personal freedom, sexual freedom and protection of private life. Article 145 of this Code enshrines voluntary interruption of pregnancy as a crime. In this chapter and concerning crimes against physical integrity, there is special relevance to the crimes of ill-treatment of minors and ill-treatment of spouse, fundamental typifications to affirm the rule of law and protect human rights in Timorese society. Also emphasised here is the criminalisation of enslavement and trafficking in persons, which arises from the humanist conception that indicated the path for the elaboration of this code. Title III gathers crimes against life in democracy, with emphasis on crimes against public tranquillity, State security and life in society, and also electoral crimes and against public authority. In this title and in crimes against life in society, special emphasis is given to the provision for specific typical crimes against the environment with punishment for conduct that causes unsustainable harm to fauna, flora and natural habitat; this shows that society's concern about the preservation of natural resources and protection of the environment has already emerged. Title IV of this Book enshrines protection of patrimony. Here the normative system gathers the legal types of crimes that are common in many penal legislations, such as theft, robbery, abuse of trust, damage. These types are defined as simple or aggravated, as circumstances such as the value and nature of the thing appropriated, the means and form of action, violence, or other circumstances that can increase the agent's fault or unlawfulness. Titles V and VI gather crimes against the administration of justice and those that the agent commits when already holding public function; here punishment is enshrined for falsity in procedural acts, various obstructions of jurisdictional activity, refusal to carry out that activity as denial of justice, and also shows, together with others, acts that constitute bribery and prevarication of magistrates, officials, lawyers or public defenders. It also criminalises activity concerning personal favouritism in justice and classic incriminations such as slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and omission of criminal participation. Concerning the exercise of public function, in general, conduct of officials that constitutes crimes of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or abuse of public force, or economic participation in business by persons exercising public office or function is also criminalised. In this matter the Penal Code broadens the concept of public official to include in that concept other analogous situations such as officials of international organisations, foreign public officials exercising activity in Timor-Leste or persons who receive appointment to perform, alone or with others, activity that falls within the scope of public administrative or jurisdictional function. In title VII crimes of falsifying documents, technical notation, currency and sealed or stamped values, weights and measures, marks, dies and stamps are typified, with various punishments, according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of each falsified object, with provision concerning seizure and loss of objects to be used to commit these crimes. Finally, in title VIII crimes against the economy are typified, with punishment for money laundering, as the doctrine that has just recently emerged concerning criminalisation of anti-economic activity, tax fraud and smuggling and embezzlement in the customs or border area. criminal punishment is maintained for disobedience to requisition that the Government makes for goods, and also conduct that can disturb, harm or impede any public act, such as the holding of public competition or judicial auction. It is also emphasised that, within this instrument, a path is sought to achieve balance for each abstract penal framework, according to the type and gravity of each crime, hierarchy in the legal interests that each norm protects and the maximum limit enshrined for imprisonment.In the legislative options that arise within this Penal Code, different treatment is given to the more serious criminality, for those which, according to the general rule, the legal type of crime provides only for imprisonment. In minor and medium criminality, as a general rule, the provision of the norm gives the court the alternative to, according to each case, use a custodial sentence or a fine, with the criminal policy orientation to recognize the fine as an autonomous penalty, not as a complementary penalty to the principal penalty. With the approval of this Code, the Timorese State has one more legal instrument, modern and adequate, to offer a better quality public-penal service and respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, magistrates, public defenders, lawyers, justice officials and also other judicial actors, who use these diplomas in their daily work, receive the obligation to undertake continuous training, the national legal order and the rule-of-law and democratic State will be strengthened further. BOOK I GENERAL PART TITLE I
APPLICATION OF PENAL LAW

SOLE CHAPTER GENERAL PRINCIPLES

Article 1 Principle of legality

1. An action or omission cannot be considered a crime before the law that already existed when that action or omission occurred defines that action or omission as a crime and establishes the respective penalty. 2. Security measures may only be applied to states of dangerousness whose presuppositions have already been defined by prior law. Article 2 Prohibition of analogy

Analogy may not be used to qualify an action or omission as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine their legal consequences. Article 3
Application of penal law in time

1. A person may not be punished for a fact that the law defines as a crime at the moment they committed that fact when a subsequent law states that that fact is no longer a crime. 2. In that case, if there is already a conviction decision, even if final, the execution and penal effects of that conviction must cease. 3. The law that existed when the crime occurred applies to prior conduct when, in the concrete case, that law favours the agent and, if there is already a decision with res judicata, it is still possible to derive some advantage from that law. Article 4
Exceptional or temporary law

Exceptional or temporary law continues to apply to facts committed during the time that law existed even though the time relating to its duration has already passed or the circumstances that determined that law have ended. Article 5
Moment at which the fact is committed

It must be considered that the agent commits the crime at the moment the action or omission occurs, regardless of the moment at which the typical result of that action or omission arises. Article 6

Place where the fact is committed
It must be considered that the agent commits the fact in the place where the action or omission occurs, in whole or in part, and also in the place where the typical result of that fact arises or should properly arise. Article 7 Principle of territoriality

Save for other provisions in international treaties or conventions and regardless of the agent's nationality, Timorese penal law applies to facts committed in the territory of Timor-Leste and inside ships or aircraft with Timorese registration or flag. Article 8
Facts committed in non-national territory

Save for other provisions in international treaties or conventions, Timorese penal law applies to facts committed in places that are not Timorese territory in the following situations:
a) When that fact constitutes a crime provided for in articles 196 to 206, 229 to 242 and 307 to 313; b) When that fact constitutes a crime provided for in articles 123 to 135, 165 to 169 and 175 to 178, provided the agent is found in Timor-Leste and they cannot be extradited or there is a decision stating they cannot be surrendered; c) When the agent commits that fact against a Timorese person, provided that agent habitually resides in Timor-Leste and they are found in Timor-Leste; d) When a Timorese person commits that fact or a foreigner commits that fact against a Timorese person, provided the agent can be found in Timor-Leste, the law in the place where that fact occurred punishes that fact and that fact constitutes a crime that admits

extradition and in that case there cannot be authorization for that extradition; e) When that fact constitutes a crime for which the Timorese State, according to an international convention or treaty, has an obligation to try. Article 9
Limitations on the application of Timorese law

1. Timorese penal law may only be applied to facts committed in non-national territory when the agent has not yet received judgment with a final decision in the place where they committed that fact or, if they have received it, they have absconded and have not served the sanction, in whole or in part. 2. Nevertheless, as the previous number, Timorese penal law will still apply, the fact will still be judged according to the law of the country where the agent committed the fact, when that law favours the agent in that case. 3. In the case provided for in the previous number, the applicable sanction will be converted to its correspondent in the Timorese penal system or, if there is no correspondence, to the one that Timorese penal law provides for that fact. 4. When the agent must receive judgment in Timor-Leste and they have already received judgment in the place where they committed the fact, the penalty they have already served abroad must also be taken into account. 5. The regime provided for in no. 2 does not apply to the crimes identified in the previous article, paragraphs a) and b). Article 10 Subsidiary application

Save for other provisions, the norms of this Code also apply to facts punished by special law. TITLE II CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PRESUPPOSITIONS
Article 11

Action and omission
1. When a result is also included in the legal type of a crime, the criminal fact encompasses the action adequate to produce that result and also the omission of the action adequate to avoid that result, save when the law has a different intention. 2. The omission that produces some result will only be punished when the omitter themselves has a legal obligation to avoid that result. 3. In the case written in the previous number, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation. Article 12 Criminal responsibility

1. Criminal responsibility for the infractions written in this Code falls only on singular persons and cannot be transmitted to other people. 2. Collective persons may only receive criminal responsibility for infractions provided for in this Code or in special law when and under the conditions that the law expressly consecrates. Article 13
Responsibility for acting in another's name

A person who acts as a holder in the organ of a collective person or de facto association or as another person's representative will be punished, even if they do not have and the person they represent does have the condition, quality or relationship that is provided for in the type of crime. Article 14 Subjective imputation

Only facts that the agent commits with intent or, in cases that the law specifically provides for, with negligence may give rise to punishment.

Article 15 Types of intent

1. A person acts with intent when, knowing in advance that a fact fulfils the type of a crime, they act with the intention to realize that fact. 2. A person also acts with intent when they know perfectly well that their behaviour will produce as a necessary consequence the fact that fulfils the type of a crime. 3. When the agent knows that their behaviour may produce the fact that fulfils the type of a crime, there is intent when the agent acts nonetheless and agrees with that behaviour. Article 16 Types of negligence

1. A person acts with negligence when, due to not having the care that, according to the circumstances, they must use and they can use:
a) They know that their behaviour may produce the fact that fulfils the type of a crime but act even though they do not agree with that behaviour; or
b) They do not know that their behaviour may realize that fact. 2. The types of negligence referred to in the previous number become gross negligence when some circumstance shows that the agent acted with recklessness or temerity, due to not following the basic care that they must follow in that case. Article 17
Error regarding circumstances

1. Error regarding an element of fact or element of law that is included in the legal type of a crime and also error regarding the prohibition that, reasonably, the agent must

know in order to be able to take awareness regarding the unlawfulness excludes intent. 2. The regime provided for in the previous number also includes error regarding the existence of presuppositions to exclude unlawfulness or presuppositions to exclude culpability. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers do not prevent the punishability of negligent conduct when the law provides for it and its presuppositions also exist.

Article 18
Error regarding unlawfulness

1. Ignorance of the law does not exclude the unlawfulness of conduct that violates that law. 2. Error regarding the unlawfulness of the fact, when unavoidable, excludes culpability. 3. When error regarding unlawfulness is avoidable, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation. Article 19 Aggravation due to result

When the penalty applicable to a fact must be aggravated according to the result that that fact produces, that aggravation only exists when that result can be imputed to the agent, at least, due to their negligence. Article 20 Inimputability due to age

1. Persons who are not yet 16 years old have penal inimputability. 2. The provisions of this diploma will also apply to young people who are already 16 years old but not yet 21 years old in situations that autonomous legislation does not regulate, regarding the application and execution of criminal sanctions. Article 21 Inimputability due to psychic anomaly

1. A person will be considered inimputable when, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment they commit the fact they cannot evaluate the unlawfulness of that fact or make a decision according to that evaluation. 2. A person may receive a declaration as inimputable when, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment they commit the fact, their capacity to evaluate the unlawfulness of that fact or to make a decision according to that evaluation is sensibly diminished. 3. The agent's proven incapacity to receive influence from the penalty may constitute an indication of the situation provided for in the previous number. 4. The agent does not lose imputability when they themselves provoke the psychic anomaly in order to commit that fact. CHAPTER II FORMS OF CRIME

Article 22 Preparatory act

Preparatory acts may not receive punishment, save for other legal provisions. Article 23 Attempt

There is an attempt when the agent begins execution of a crime that they have already made the decision to commit, and commits, in whole or only in part, the acts that can objectively produce its result and that result does not arise due only to circumstances that have no relation to the agent's will. Article 24 Punishability of attempt

1. In intentional crimes that may give rise to imprisonment with a maximum limit higher than 3 years and in cases that the law determines, the attempt may give rise to punishment. 2. Save for other provisions, the attempt gives rise to the penalty that will be applied to the consummated crime, with extraordinary mitigation. Article 25 Non-punishable attempt

The attempt may not receive punishment when it can be seen clearly that the means that the agent used have no aptitude or the object that is essential for the consummation of the crime does not exist. Article 26 Voluntary desistance

The agent's attempt may not be punished when they voluntarily desist from continuing the execution of the crime, prevent the crime from being consummated or its result from arising or make every effort to prevent the consummation or that result. Article 27
In participation

When there is participation, the agent's attempt does not give rise to punishment when they voluntarily prevent the crime from being consummated or the result of that crime from arising, or make every effort to prevent the consummation or that result, even though the other participants continue to execute or already consummate that fact. Article 28 Subsequent repentance

In crimes that do not have violence or serious threat against a person, the penalty will receive extraordinary mitigation or, according to the circumstances, the agent will receive exemption from the penalty, when the damage has already been repaired, the thing has already been returned or the situation

has already been legalized before there is participation or the competent authority receives a denunciation or complaint. CHAPTER III AGENTS OF CRIME

Article 29 Agent

Participation in committing a crime may be as authorship, instigation or complicity and many people may participate together in only one fact

Article 30 Authorship

1. A person who executes the fact themselves or uses another person to execute that fact will be considered an author. 2. A person who, according to a tacit or express agreement, takes direct part in the execution or acts together with another person, combining their effort with theirs, to commit only one crime will be considered a co-author

Article 31 Instigation

A person who convinces, directly and intentionally, another person to commit a crime will be punished as an author, provided that crime is already executed or its execution has already begun. Article 32 Complicity

1. A person who, intentionally, gives material or moral help to another person to commit some crime will be punished as an accomplice. 2. The penalty that is applied to the type of unlawful act will be applied to the accomplice, with extraordinary mitigation. Article 33

Culpability in participation
Each participant will be punished according to their culpability, regardless of the punishment of the other participants or the extent to which the other participants have culpability. Article 34 Unlawfulness in participation

1. When the unlawfulness of the fact or the degree of that unlawfulness depends on some special quality or relationship of the agent, it is enough that one participant has that quality or relationship in order to give rise to applying the respective penalty to all the participants, save when the incriminating norm has a different intention. 2. When, due to the rule that the previous number provides for, a heavier penalty must be applied to some participant, considering the circumstances that characterize that case, a different penalty may be put in place of a certain penalty that, if that rule did not exist, would be applied to them. CHAPTER IV CONCURRENCE AND CONTINUED CRIME

Article 35 Crimes in concurrence

1. To know how many crimes the agent committed, it is necessary to count how many types of crime they have already committed or how many times their conduct fulfils the type of only one crime. 2. For the provisions of the following article, the situation in which the agent, having committed one crime, commits another crime again before receiving a conviction with a decision final in judgment will be considered as concurrence. Article 36 Punishment in concurrence

1. When several crimes are in concurrence, only one penalty must be applied, whose minimum limit is the highest concrete penalty among all the concrete penalties applied to each crime and whose maximum limit is the material sum of all the partial penalties
2. In concurrence, the maximum limit of the framework may not exceed 600 days for the fine penalty or 30 years for imprisonment, even in situations where the material sum of the partial penalties exceeds that legal limit. 3. To determine the single penalty, the court must consider, together, the facts and the agent's personality. Article 37 Sanctions in concurrence

1. When some applied penalties are fines and some are imprisonment, the nature of each penalty will remain as it is. 2. Accessory penalties and security measures, even though only one of the applicable laws or only one of the previous decisions provides for them, will be maintained. Article 38
Suspended sentence in legal cumulation

1. Imprisonment with suspended execution may only be included in cumulation with other imprisonment penalties when:
a) All those penalties are imprisonment with suspended execution and that cumulation does not prevent the execution of the single penalty from being suspended; b) If that cumulation is done with effective imprisonment, there are circumstances that give rise to revoking the suspension of the execution of that imprisonment, even without that cumulation; c) The periods for suspension of the suspended sentences to be cumulated are not the same, or, if those periods are the same, each one's compliance is in different phases, and the court establishes only one period for suspension, according to the necessity of prevention and the circumstances that characterize that case

Article 39
Concurrence with supervening knowledge

When a decision has already obtained res judicata but, before that penalty receives compliance, prescription or extinction, there is knowledge that the agent is in some situation that the previous articles describe, the rules that are written in them will be applied. Article 40
Crime with other infractions

When one fact constitutes a crime and a contra-ordenação at the same time, the agent will be punished for the crime, but the accessory sanction provided for the other infractions will also be applied. Article 41 Continued crime

1. A person who commits several times the same type of crime or commits different types of crime that fundamentally protect only one legal good, with essentially homogeneous behaviour and in only one external situation that considerably diminishes their culpability, commits only one continued crime,
save for crimes that protect eminently personal goods. 2. The penalty that will be applied to the behaviour that is the most serious among the others that are included in that continuation will be applied to the continued crime. Article 42
Several norms in concurrence

For facts that several legal provisions qualify, in whole or only in part, as a crime and that are not included in the situations that the previous articles describe, only one incriminating norm will be applied according to the following rules:
a) The special norm will be applied with prejudice to the general norm;

b) Preference will be given to the subsidiary norm over the principal norm; c) The norm that is broader and more complex consumes the norm that provides for facts subsumable in its provision. CHAPTER V CAUSES OF EXCLUSION

SECTION I
CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE UNLAWFULNESS

Article 43 Exclusion of unlawfulness

1. A fact may not give rise to punishment as a crime when the legal order considered as a totality has removed its unlawfulness. 2. In particular, facts that a person commits when exercising their right, in legitimate defence, in a state of justifying necessity or with consent regarding a freely available legal interest are not unlawful. Article 44 Legitimate defence

The agent's action necessary to prevent an illegitimate, imminent or already executing but not yet finished aggression against the legal interests protected by the legal order of the agent or another person will be considered as legitimate defence. Article 45
State of justifying necessity

Facts that the agent commits as a means to prevent a current danger that threatens the legally protected interests of that agent or another person are not unlawful, when the following requirements exist:

a) The interest to be protected has sensible superiority when compared with the sacrificed interest; and
b) It is reasonable to sacrifice the injured party's interest when considering the nature or value of the interest that is currently facing danger. Article 46 Conflict of duties

1. The agent's behaviour is not unlawful when, when there is a conflict in complying with several legal duties or legitimate orders of authority, they comply with the duty or order with a value equal to or greater than the duty or order that they sacrifice. 2. The duty of hierarchical obedience ends when it gives rise to a crime. Article 47 Consent

1. Together with the cases that the law specifically provides for, consent excludes the unlawfulness of the fact when it concerns a freely available legal interest and that fact does not offend good customs. 2. Consent may be shown by any means that expresses the serious, free and clarified will of the holder of the legally protected interest, and may be revoked according to that holder's wish until the execution of the fact. 3. Consent only has efficacy when it comes from a person who is older than 16 years and has the necessary discernment to evaluate the meaning and scope of that consent at the moment they give that consent. 4. When the agent does not know that that consent exists, they will be punished with the penalty that applies to the attempt.

5. Presumed consent will be equated to effective consent, which exists when the situation in which the agent acts gives rise to thinking reasonably that, if the holder of the legally protected interest knew the circumstances in which the agent commits that fact, they would already give consent effectively to that fact. SECTION II
CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE CULPABILITY

Article 48
Excess in legitimate defence

1. Means that, due to their species and degree in their utilization, become excessive for the agent's action to defend themselves may give rise to extraordinary mitigation in the penalty that will be applied to the crime. 2. Excess in the means used in legitimate defence due to non-censurable disturbance, fear or shock excludes the agent's culpability. Article 49
State of exculpating necessity

1. A person who commits an unlawful fact adequate to prevent a current danger that threatens the life, physical integrity, honour or liberty of the agent or another person acts without culpability, when there is no other way to remove that danger and, according to the circumstances that characterize that case, it is not reasonable to request other behaviour from the agent. 2. When the danger that threatens legal interests that the previous article does not mention and the other presuppositions that are written in it all arise, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation or, exceptionally, the agent may receive exemption from the penalty. Article 50 Exculpating undue obedience

A civil servant who complies with an order acts without culpability when they do not know that that order makes them commit some crime and the circumstances that they see currently do not show clearly that their behaviour is a crime. TITLE III CIRCUMSTANCES

SOLE CHAPTER GENERAL RULES

Article 51 Measure of penalty

1. To determine the measure of the penalty, according to the limits that the law defines, it is necessary to consider the agent's culpability and the necessity to do prevention. 2. To determine the concrete penalty, the court considers all the circumstances that are not included in the type of crime and favour or disfavour the agent. 3. In the sentence, the grounds for the measure of the penalty will be expressly referred to. Article 52 General aggravating circumstances

1. Prior, contemporaneous or subsequent circumstances that are not included in the legal type and show that the unlawfulness of the fact, the agent's conduct or the agent's culpability is stronger and increase the necessity of the penalty will be considered as general circumstances that aggravate the agent's responsibility. 2. The following may constitute general aggravating circumstances, together with others:
a) The agent executes the crime with disloyalty, which exists when the agent uses treason, ambush, waiting, disguise; b) The agent commits the crime against a person with means, mode or form that, directly or indirectly,

guarantee that they will not face danger that may come from the defence that the victim may present; c) The agent commits the crime with fraud, deceit, excess of power or authority or takes advantage of circumstances regarding place, time to commit that crime; d) The agent receives payment, value or some reward to commit that crime; e) The agent commits that crime due to racist motivation or discriminatory sentiment regarding the victim's gender, ideology, religion, ethnicity, nationality, sex or sexual orientation, illness or physical diminishment; f) The agent has a special obligation not to commit that crime, to prevent that crime or to participate in punishing that crime or uses public authority that they have or they say they have to commit that crime; g) In cases where there is no recidivism, the agent commits one or more crimes with the same nature within the previous 3 years starting from the moment they commit the crime for which they currently face judgment, independent of the moment they receive conviction; h) The agent commits that crime on the occasion that they commit another crime to make it easier to commit one or several crimes; i) The agent commits that crime with facility because they enter or try to enter the victim's residence, use poison, flood, fire, explosion, shipwreck or breakdown in a boat or weapon; j) The participation of two or more people gives facility to commit that crime or take advantage of the consequences of that crime; k) The agent intentionally and inhumanly increases the victim's suffering, by producing suffering that is not necessary to consummate that crime or other acts regarding spoliation, cruelty or destruction that are not necessary to commit the crime; l) The victim is the agent's spouse or person who lives with the agent as wife with husband at that time or before, or their ascendant, descendant, sibling, adoptee or adopter; m) The victim is a very vulnerable person due to their age, illness or physical or psychic deficiency, when that circumstance is not included in the legal type. Article 53 Recidivism

1. A person who, having received conviction with a sentence final in judgment in effective imprisonment higher than 6 months, they alone or together with another person, in any way, commits an intentional crime for which gives rise to effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months will be punished as a recidivist, when, according to the circumstances that characterize the case, the agent must receive censure because the conviction or convictions that have already fallen on them were not enough to prevent them from committing another crime. 2. There is no recidivism when, from the moment the agent commits one crime until the moment they commit another crime and not counting for that period the time in which they are serving a procedural measure, penalty or security measure that removes liberty, 4 years have already passed. 3. When there is recidivism, the minimum limit of the applicable penalty will rise by one third, but its maximum limit does not change and that aggravation may not be higher than the highest penalty that has already been applied in the previous convictions. Article 54 Criminal habituality

1. When the agent commits an intentional crime that, concretely, gives rise to effective imprisonment higher than 1 year in the following circumstances cumulatively:
a) The agent has already committed before three or more intentional crimes that give rise to effective imprisonment;

b) From each of these crimes until the next crime, 3 years have not yet passed; c) The evaluation of the facts and the agent's personality together shows that the agent has a tendency to commit crimes; the penalty regarding the crime committed will be applied with its minimum and maximum limits having risen by one third. 2. This provision prevails over the rule of punishment due to recidivism itself. Article 55 General mitigating circumstances

1. Circumstances that arise before the agent commits the crime, arise together with the crime or after the crime has passed and favour the agent will be considered general mitigating circumstances for the agent's responsibility. 2. The following circumstances may become general mitigating circumstances, together with others:
a) The causes of exclusion that are written in the previous chapter when the requirements provided for the causes of exclusion to produce some effect do not exist; b) The agent commits the fact because some fact produces violent emotion, obsession or passionate state with a nature similar to these or reacts immediately to provocation; c) The agent presents themselves to the authority before they know that there is a criminal proceeding against them; d) The agent confesses the crime that they committed themselves or gives a decisive contribution to knowing the circumstances that characterize that criminal action; e) There are acts that show that the agent already repents; f) The agent's intent or negligence is diminished; g) The victim and agent are reconciled. Article 56
Circumstances for extraordinary mitigation

1. Together with the cases that the law expressly provides for, the penalty provided for in the legal type will be extraordinarily mitigated when there are circumstances that arise before the agent commits the crime, arise together with the crime or after the crime has passed and, together or alone, markedly diminish the unlawfulness of the conduct, the agent's culpability or the necessity of the penalty. 2. For the effect of the previous number, the following circumstances will be considered, together with others:
a) The agent acts due to serious threat or due to receiving influence from a person on whom they depend or whom they must obey; b) The agent acts due to honourable motive, because the victim themselves requested or tempted them or due to unjust provocation or offence that they did not deserve; c) The agent repairs the prejudice that they produced or reduces the effects of that prejudice, at any moment but before judgment begins; d) The crime occurred a long time ago and the agent has had good behaviour continuously; e) The agent has imputability sensibly diminished. Article 57
Degree of extraordinary mitigation

1. When there is extraordinary mitigation of the penalty, the following rules will be applied to the limits of the applicable penalty:
a) The maximum limit of imprisonment will fall by one third; b) The minimum limit of imprisonment will fall by one fifth when it is equal to or higher than 3 years and falls to the legal minimum when it is less than 3 years; c) The maximum limit of the fine will fall by one third and its minimum limit will fall to the legal minimum; d) When the maximum limit of imprisonment is not higher than 3 years, a fine may be put in place of imprisonment, within the general limits. 2. The concrete penalty that results from extraordinary mitigation may receive substitution, together with suspension, according to the general rules.

Article 58 Concurrence of circumstances

1. When some circumstance that modifies the abstract framework of the legal type arises, the regime of extraordinary mitigation that is written in the previous article will be applied. 2. When two or more circumstances that modify the abstract framework of the legal type arise together, only one will be considered according to the provisions of the previous article, the others will be considered as general mitigating circumstances to determine the measure of the penalty. TITLE IV
LEGAL CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article 59
Penalties and security measures
1.The death penalty or penalties or security measures that deprive liberty with a perpetual character or unlimited or indefinite duration shall not be applied. 2. For a single act, a penalty and a security measure that deprives liberty shall not be applied together. Article 60
Limits of penalties and security measures

1. The concrete penalty shall not be more severe than the seriousness of the guilt. 2. Security measures based on the dangerousness of the agent who committed the act provided for as a crime shall last until it is seen that the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to the measure has ceased and its duration shall not be longer than the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the said crime. Article 61
Purpose of penalties and security measures36

36 (61) Purpose (s) - objective

Penalties and security measures shall be applied to protect legal interests essential to life in society and to achieve the agent's reintegration into society. Article 62
Choice of penalty and security measure

1. When either a penalty that deprives liberty or a penalty that does not deprive liberty may be applied to a crime, the court shall choose the latter when that penalty is better and sufficient to achieve the purpose of the penalty. 2. To choose a security measure to apply to an agent whose dangerousness has already been shown in the proceedings, the agent's personality and the treatment that is best for the situation must be considered. Article 63
Effects of penalties and security measures

A penalty or security measure shall not, as a necessary effect, extinguish civil, professional or political rights. Article 64
Execution of penalties or measures that deprive liberty

1. An agent who receives a conviction with an effective prison sentence or is subject to an internment measure may obtain conditional release or release on probation. 2. Save for other provisions, conditional release must be granted to a convict who has already served two-fifths of the sentence. 3. The convict's consent must be obtained to grant conditional release to him or her, except in the situation provided for in the previous number. 4. Only the law shall establish the prerequisites and conditions for granting conditional release and also the rights and obligations of the released persons and the prerequisites and conditions for executing effective imprisonment in an open regime. Article 65

Concurrence of penalties and measures that deprive liberty
1. When an agent receives a conviction to an effective prison sentence and is subject to an internment measure, the measure must be executed first and discounted from the prison sentence. 2. Should the internment measure end, the court shall place the agent on conditional release when he or she has already served the time corresponding to part of the sentence and his or her release appears compatible37 with the defense of legal order and social peace. CHAPTER II PRISON SENTENCE

Article 66
Duration of prison sentence

1. A prison sentence must last at least 30 days and shall not exceed 25 years. 2. In cases that the law specifically provides for, a prison sentence may last up to 30 years. 3. The maximum limit of the sentence referred to in the previous number shall not be exceeded. Article 67
Substitution of imprisonment with fine

1. In place of an applied prison sentence not exceeding 1 year, a fine may be imposed for the same period, up to the maximum legal limit, when the need to prevent future crime does not require the serving of imprisonment and, according to the circumstances surrounding the case, the court sees that the execution should not be suspended. 2. When the convict does not pay the substitute fine or any installment thereof, he or she must still serve the prison sentence that was first fixed, but the convict may still pay the fine in full or show property to guarantee

37 (65) - Compatible; this goes well with (something else); that which does not prejudice (something else). Compatible with the defense of social peace = does not prejudice the defense of social peace

the payment to halt the serving, discounting the installments that have already been paid. 3. The court must give reasons for its decision not to make the substitution when the applied penalty gives rise to the substitution. Article 68
Suspension of execution of imprisonment

1. The court may suspend the execution of an applied prison sentence not exceeding 3 years, for 1 to 5 years, beginning from the transit of the decision in res judicata. 2. The grounds for the suspension must be included in the decision, namely, the agent's personality, the circumstances surrounding the commission of the crime, his or her previous conduct, conditions concerning his or her life and, especially, the predictability concerning his or her future conduct. 3. The court must give reasons for its decision not to suspend the execution when the applied penalty gives rise to the suspension. Article 69
Suspension of imprisonment with duty

1. To suspend the execution of a prison sentence, the court, as a condition, may order the convict to fulfill some duty, not humiliating, to repair the harm of the crime. 2. The following duties may serve as a condition for the suspension:
a) The convict repairs or gives a guarantee to repair the damage that the crime caused, within a determined period; b) The convict presents an apology to the victim in public; c) The convict performs some task related to the crime committed; d) The convict delivers money to the State or to a charitable institution with significance for the convict's reintegration. 3. An obligation that orders him or her to do something that cannot reasonably38 be asked of him or her to do shall not be applied to the convict. 4. Until the period for the suspension ends, the obligation that is being applied to the convict may be changed when some supervening relevant circumstance arises or has passed that the court only now learns of some relevant circumstance. 5. The provision of n. 2 of the previous article also applies here. Article 70 Rules of conduct

1. The court may order the convict to comply, during the suspension period, with rules of conduct that facilitate his or her integration into society, namely:
a) Not to exercise some profession; b) Not to frequent certain environments or places; c) Not to reside in certain places or regions; d) Not to associate with certain persons, to let certain persons stay in his or her home or to receive certain persons; e) Not to frequent certain associations or participate in certain meetings; f) Not to possess objects that may facilitate his or her commission of crime; g) To present himself or herself to the court, social reintegration technicians or non-police entities periodically. 2. Having obtained the convict's consent, the court may also order him or her to undergo medical treatment or cure in an adequate institution. 3. The provisions of n. 3 to 5 of the previous article also apply here. Article 71
Suspension of imprisonment with supervision

1. When simple or conditional suspension of imprisonment is not sufficient to guarantee the delinquent's recovery and to dissuade him or her from criminal activity, the court may apply the suspension together with supervision by the social reintegration service for the convict during the suspension period, having obtained the convict's agreement, when possible. 2. The social reintegration service together with the Public Prosecutor and the judge who gave the conviction, having heard the convict, shall elaborate a plan for social reintegration, which, having obtained approval from the court, shall be fulfilled with the assistance of an official or the reintegration service. 3. All obligations that are applied to the convict must be included in the readaptation plan; the court may order the convict to comply with the obligations and rules of conduct that are written in articles 69 and 70 or other obligations that are of interest to the readaptation plan, namely:
a) To obtain treatment or internment in an adequate establishment, when circumstances require; b) To comply with the summons of the magistrate responsible for execution or social reintegration technicians; c) To receive visits from social reintegration technicians and to give information to the technician concerning his or her means of living; d) To notify the social reintegration technician concerning changes to his or her residence or employment, and also concerning absences from his or her residence when longer than 8 days; e) To obtain prior authorization from the magistrate responsible for execution before leaving for abroad. 4. The provision of article 68, n. 2, also applies here. Article 72
Modification of the suspension regime

When during the time that the execution of the prison sentence is suspended the convict does not fulfill the obligation that is being applied to him or her or receives judgment and conviction for another crime, the court, considering the circumstances in which, may alter the regime that was fixed for the suspension of the execution of the prison sentence, modify the obligation or give a solemn warning to the convict, according to the circumstances of each case. Article 73 Revocation of suspension

1. When during the time that the execution of the prison sentence is suspended the convict receives judgment and conviction for another crime or intentionally relapses into non-compliance with the rule or obligation that is being applied to him or her, and it is seen that modification of the suspension regime cannot be made or the modification is not sufficient, the court shall revoke the suspension. 2. The suspension of the execution of the prison sentence must be revoked when, during the respective period, the convict commits an intentional crime for which he or she receives a conviction with effective imprisonment. 3. The revocation of the suspension does not give the convict the right to receive back the installments that he or she has already made during the suspension and because of the suspension. Article 74
Extinction of prison sentence
When the suspension of the execution of the prison sentence is not revoked, after the respective period has passed, the sentence and its effects shall be extinguished

CHAPTER III FINE PENALTY

Article 75
Duration of fine penalty

1. A fine penalty shall be fixed with its minimum at 10 days and its maximum at 360 days, save for other provisions. 2. One day of fine corresponds to an amount, which the court fixes according to the convict's economic and financial situation and his or her personal burdens, within the range of one American dollar to 200 American dollars. 3. When the circumstances surrounding the case justify, the court may authorize payment of the fine within a period of 1 year, or allow payment by installments39, but the last installment must be paid within 2 years beginning from the day that the conviction transits in res judicata. Article 76
Alternative imprisonment for fine penalty

The decision that directly applies a fine penalty shall also fix alternative imprisonment, for the time that corresponds to the fine, reduced by two-thirds. Article 77
Reduction or exemption of fine penalty

1. When a convict with a fine penalty does not fulfill the penalty because of circumstances that arose after the conviction and make impossible or difficult the fulfillment of the fine, but are not imputable to him or her, the court may decree reduction or exemption from the penalty. 2. The provision of the previous number also applies to a fine penalty that substitutes imprisonment. CHAPTER IV
COMMUNITY SERVICE PENALTY

Article 78 Community service

1. A community service penalty is one in which the convict must perform unpaid service for a public body or other entity that the court considers to be in the public interest, namely having obtained the convict's consent. 2. The court shall fix the time for the convict to perform the service and, for this, shall convert the imprisonment fixed in the sentence at one day per one hour of service, but shall not exceed 240 hours. 3. The work may be performed within normal service hours or not, continuously or not, but on each day

39 (75) Installment (s) – each amount that must be paid within a period to pay a debt or burden (obligation or onus).

shall not be longer than the time that is established in the regime for extraordinary hours and shall not prejudice the survival40 of the convict or the convict's family. 4. Unjustified non-compliance in performing community service gives rise to serving the penalty that was first applied, discounting the days on which the work was already performed, according to the provision of n. 2. Article 79 Requirement

1. The court may apply a community service penalty in place of a prison sentence not longer than 1 year or a fine penalty when it sees that by this means the purpose of punishment can be adequately and sufficiently realized and, concerning imprisonment, reasons concerning criminal prevention show that it is better not to suspend the execution or substitute with a fine. 2. The convict's consent must be obtained to apply community service, which, concerning a substitute fine, may be decreed in the sentence or in a subsequent order, namely when the convict presents a request before there is an order for seizure in the execution proceedings for non-payment of the fine. 3. The provision of article 67, n. 3, shall apply here. Article 80
Suspension, reduction or exemption of penalty

When the convict does not perform the work, because of circumstances that arose after its application and make impossible or difficult the fulfillment and cannot be imputed to him or her, the regime provided for in article 77 shall apply. Article 81 Complementary legislation

40 (78) Survival (s) – minimum material condition that is needed to live. A special diploma shall regulate the other conditions for applying the community service penalty and the functioning of the service to execute the work. CHAPTER V ADMONISHMENT PENALTY

Article 82 Admonishment

When considering the agent guilty of committing a crime that gives rise to an abstract prison sentence not exceeding 3 years or a fine, the court may give only an admonishment to him or her, when, cumulatively:
a) The damage of the criminal conduct has already been repaired; b) The agent is a first-time delinquent; c) The admonishment is sufficient to achieve criminal prevention and to recover the delinquent. Article 83
Execution of admonishment penalty

The court shall make the admonishment, with a solemn and adequate oral reprimand to the convict, at a public hearing, when the decision that applies the penalty has already transited in res judicata. CHAPTER VI ACCESSORY PENALTY

Article 84 General principle

1. The law may establish for some crime a prohibition to exercise some right or profession. 2. An accessory penalty shall be cumulated with other accessory penalties, must be applied together with the principal penalty and its duration must be fixed according to the seriousness of the guilt. Article 85
Temporary suspension to exercise public function

1. When convicting a person who exercises a public office with effective imprisonment, the court must decree suspension of the exercise of that function during the period that he or she serves the sentence, when the convict does not receive disciplinary dismissal
2. The effects that, according to the respective law, accompany the disciplinary sanction of suspension to exercise the function shall be applied to the convict, during the suspension period. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers shall apply to professions or activities that require a public title, authorization or approval by a public authority to exercise. Article 86 Prohibition to exercise function

1. A holder of public office, public official or agent of the Administration who, when exercising the activity for which he or she obtained election or appointment to hold, receives a conviction with a prison sentence exceeding 3 years may receive a prohibition to exercise those functions, for 2 to 5 years, when the following circumstances arise:
a) The agent committed the act with flagrant and serious abuse of his or her function or with manifest and serious violation of the duty that he or she must fulfill in the function; b) The agent shows manifest unworthiness or incapacity to exercise the office; c) The nature of the act destroys the trust that must exist to exercise the function. 2. The provision of the previous number also applies to professions or activities that a person must have a public title, authorization or approval by a public authority to exercise. 3. The time in which the accused is deprived of his or her liberty because of a coercive measure, penalty or security measure shall not count as a period of prohibition. 4. The accessory penalty provided for in this article shall not be applied when, because of these very acts, the security measure provided for in article 100 is already being applied. 5. When applying the provisions of this article, the court's conviction must be notified to the public authority on which the official depends. Article 87 Expulsion

1. A foreign citizen who receives a conviction for committing a crime that gives rise to imprisonment exceeding 3 years and has not yet resided in Timor-Leste for 15 years may receive expulsion from the national territory:
a) For a period up to 2 years, when he or she has already resided here for more than 10 years; b) For a period up to 5 years, when he or she has already resided here for more than 5 years but not yet 10 years; c) For a period up to 10 years, when he or she has not yet resided here for 5 years. 2. The expulsion referred to in the previous number shall be applied when in the concrete case it is necessary for internal security, public health or to prevent criminal activity from continuing. 3. The total or partial serving of the principal penalty shall not be considered to execute the expulsion penalty. Article 88 Prohibition of driving

1. Disqualification from driving a motor vehicle for 3 months to 2 years may be applied to a person who receives a conviction:
a) For a crime provided for in articles 207 to 209; b) For a crime that was committed with a motorized vehicle and executed with facility because of using a motorized vehicle; c) For a crime of disobedience for refusing to take a test that the law establishes to know whether a person is driving a motorized vehicle under the disturbing effect of alcohol, psychotropic substances or products with analogous effect on his or her physical, mental
or psychological aptitude. 2. The provision of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. Article 89
Cancellation of license to possess weapons

1. When there is a conviction for an intentional crime committed with a weapon, considering the circumstances and the seriousness of the conduct, the court may decree cancellation41 of the license to use and possess weapons, for 2 to 8 years. 2. The provision of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. CHAPTER VII
DETERMINATION OF PENALTY

Article 90 General principle

1. When the law fixes a penalty, the penalty refers to the crime in its consummated form. 2. To obtain the abstract penalty framework from which the concrete penalty shall be determined:
a) The modifying circumstances of recidivism and criminal habituality provided for in articles 53 and 54, respectively, shall be applied to the abstract penalty of the consummated crime; b) Following the provision of the previous paragraph or starting from the abstract penalty of the consummated crime, when there are modifying circumstances, the situation that gives rise to extraordinary mitigation, when it exists, shall be considered. Article 91
