Concrete determination of penalty

1. Having obtained the abstract penalty framework according to the previous article, the court shall assess all circumstances that do not enter into the legal type and do not enter into the valuation as provisions of the previous article but aggravate or diminish the convict's responsibility. 2. Based on the valuation of the latter circumstances, the court shall fix the exact measure of the penalty that it considers necessary to protect legal interests essential to life in

41 (89) Cancellation (s) - Annulment

society and for the agent's social reintegration, within the framework that is fixed within the limits of the type or within the limits that result from the provisions of the previous article. 3. The severity of the penalty applied to the convict may never exceed the seriousness of the guilt. Article 92
Special case of fine penalty

To apply a fine penalty, the provisions of this chapter shall be applied to determine the time concerning the duration of the fine, without prejudice to the provision of article 75, n. 2, to calculate the amount that corresponds to the fine for each day. CHAPTER VIII SECURITY MEASURE

SECTION I INTERNMENT MEASURE

Article 93 Prerequisite

When a person who is not imputable according to the provisions of article 21 commits an act described in the legal type of a crime, the court may order his or her admission to an adequate establishment, when, because of his or her psychic anomaly and the nature and seriousness of the committed act, there is a basis to fear that he or she may commit another typical act corresponding to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger. Article 94 Duration

1. When the act that the imputable person committed gives rise to imprisonment up to 3 years, the internment shall not be longer than 1 year. 2. When the imputable person committed an act that corresponds to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger punishable with a prison sentence of 5 years or more, the internment shall not be shorter

than 3 years, except when it is seen that release does not prejudice the defense of public order and social peace. 3. When the imputable person committed a crime punishable with a (prison) sentence exceeding 8 years and it is better not to release him or her because otherwise he or she may again commit a crime like this one, the internment may be extended by a further period of 2 years, successively42, until it is seen that the state of dangerousness that gave rise to the extension has ceased. 4. The internment shall not be longer than the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the crime that the accused committed. Article 95 Cessation of measure

1. The internment measure shall end when the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to the measure has ceased or, should the dangerousness continue, when the maximum duration of the measure has already been reached, except in the circumstances that are written in article 94, n. 3 and 4. 2. A review of the internment measure shall be made annually. 3. When there is an invocation that there is a justifying cause for the internment to end, the court, at a given moment, shall appreciate the question. Article 96
Substitution of internment measure

1. Release on probation or, concerning a foreigner, expulsion from the national territory may be imposed in place of the internment measure. 2. The provisions of article 87 shall apply to expulsion from the national territory. Article 97 Release on probation

1. When in the review referred to in article 95 it is seen that there is reason to believe that the purpose of the measure can be achieved in an open environment, the court shall place the internee on release on probation. 42 (94) Successively (adv) – One after another; repeatedly. 2. The period of release on probation shall be fixed at a minimum of 2 years to a maximum of 5 years, but shall not exceed the maximum limit fixed in article 94, n. 4. 3. The decision concerning release on probation may order the internee to follow rules of conduct, necessary to prevent dangerousness, as written in article 70, to follow treatment and an appropriate43 outpatient cure regime and to present himself or herself for examination and observation at the place that is indicated to him or her. 4. The agent whose execution of his or her internment has been suspended shall be placed under the surveillance of the social reintegration service. 5. When there is no reason to revoke the release on probation, after the period of release on probation has ended, the internment measure shall be declared extinct. 6. When at the moment that the period of release on probation ends a proceeding or incident that may give rise to revocation of the measure is ongoing, after the proceeding or incident ends and there is no revocation, the measure may be declared extinct. Article 98
Revocation of release on probation

1. Release on probation shall be revoked when:
a) The imputable person's conduct shows that it is necessary to apply internment; b) The non-imputable person receives a conviction to a penalty that deprives liberty and there are no prerequisites to suspend the execution of the penalty, according to article 68, n. 1. 2. Revocation gives rise to re-internment, and here the provisions of articles 94 and 96 shall also apply. Article 99
Suspension of execution of internment

1. The court may order the suspension of internment when, according to reason, it can be expected that with the suspension the purpose of the security measure can be achieved and the suspension is compatible with the defense of public order and social peace. 43 (97) Appropriate (adj) – Good for a situation. 2. Article 97, n. 3 and 4, shall also apply to the suspension of internment. 3. The execution of internment shall not be suspended when the agent receives a conviction to a penalty that deprives liberty and there are no requirements to suspend the execution of the penalty as in article 68, n. 1. 4. The duration and cessation of internment shall be determined according to articles 94 and 95, respectively44. 5. The provisions of article 98 shall also apply to the decision that suspends the internment measure. SECTION II
OTHER SECURITY MEASURES

Article 100
Professional interdiction measure

1. When a non-imputable person because of psychic anomaly commits some act provided for in the legal type of a crime related to the professional activity that he or she exercises and there is fear that, during the time that he or she exercises that occupation, he or she may commit an act like this one, the court, considering the circumstances surrounding the case and the non-imputable person's personality, may prohibit him or her from exercising the activity for 1 to 5 years. 2. The period shall be suspended during the time that the agent does not have liberty because of a procedural coercive measure, penalty or security measure. Article 101
Prohibition of driving and cancellation of license to use and possess weapons

1. A non-imputable person who commits some act provided for in article 88, n. 1, may receive disqualification from driving a motor vehicle for 2 to 6 years, when his or her personality

44 (99) Respectively (adv) – each to each; according to the order that is shown. The duration and cessation of internment shall be determined according to articles 94 and 95, respectively = The duration of internment according to article 94 and its cessation shall be determined according to article 95. causes fear that he or she may again commit an act like this one. 2. When the act that the non-imputable person committed is in relation to the use of weapons, the court may decree cancellation of the license to use and possess weapons for 5 to 10 years, when his or her personality causes fear that he or she may again commit an act like this one. 3. The provision of article 86, n. 3, shall also apply here. CHAPTER IX
OTHER CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

Article 102 Loss of object of crime

1. Objects that were used or to be used to commit a crime, or came from a crime, shall be declared lost to the State, when, according to their nature or the circumstances surrounding the case, they pose a danger to the safety of persons or to public order, or may be used again to commit a new crime. 2. Victims and third parties who did not contribute to the use or production of these objects or derive advantage from objects that are their property shall still guarantee their rights. 3. The court shall give a destination to the objects that have already been declared lost, when the law does not say anything, such as ordering the destruction of these objects or making them no longer enter into commerce. 4. The provision of n. 1 shall still apply, even if a person does not receive a penalty for the act. Article 103 Loss of advantage

1. Things, rights or advantages that were obtained, directly or indirectly, from the commission of a crime shall be declared lost to the State, without prejudice to the rights of the victim or third parties in good faith. 2. When the things, rights or advantages themselves cannot come into the hands of the State, the value of the things, rights or advantages shall be paid to the State. Article 104 Civil liability for crime

1. The court ex officio must ascertain and fix compensation for loss and damage that came from the crime when it is already known that there is damage and what the value of the damage is, except when the injured party declares, according to the criminal procedural law, that he or she wants to make a separate claim. 2. The norms of civil law shall regulate the prerequisites and calculation of compensation. 3. The person responsible for compensation may make a settlement concerning the compensation, but the settlement must be notified to the court, otherwise the act has no efficacy. Article 105
Privilege of the injured party's credit

The credit that arises from the injured party's right to compensation for loss and damage that came from the crime has preference over other credits that arose after the commission of the act, including45 costs and the amount46 concerning the fine. TITLE V RIGHT TO COMPLAINT

Article 106 Nature of crime

1. Concerning the right to make a complaint, crimes may be of a public or semi-public nature. 2. Public crimes are those that do not require a complaint to give rise to criminal proceedings. 3. Semi-public crimes are those that require a complaint to give rise to criminal proceedings. 4. A holder exercises his or her right to complaint when he or she shows that he or she wants criminal proceedings. 45 (105) Including (v) – Counted in all of this; also counted in this; entered in all of this; also entered in this. 46 (105) Amount (s) – A sum of money. Article 107
Holder of the right to complaint

When a complaint is required to conduct criminal proceedings, the persons that the criminal procedural law indicates as holders of the right to complaint have standing to present the complaint. Article 108 Period to exercise the right

The right to complaint must be exercised within 6 months and the period shall count separately for each holder of the right. Article 109
Renunciation and desistance of complaint

The renunciation, desistance or non-exercise of the right to complaint concerning one participant in the crime benefits the other participants in cases where they also cannot be subject to criminal prosecution before there is a complaint. TITLE VI EXTINCTION OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY

CHAPTER I
PRESCRIPTION OF CRIMINAL PROCEEDINGS

Article 110 Period of prescription

1. Criminal proceedings shall be extinguished, because of prescription, when, beginning from the moment that the agent committed the crime, the following periods have already passed:
a) 20 years, when concerning a crime punishable with a prison sentence with a maximum limit exceeding 12 years; b) 15 years, when concerning a crime punishable with a prison sentence with a maximum limit exceeding 7 years but not reaching 12 years; c) 8 years, when concerning a crime punishable with a prison sentence with a maximum limit exceeding 3 years but not exceeding 7 years; d) 4 years, in other cases. 2. When the law establishes as an alternative47 for a crime a prison sentence or a fine penalty, the former shall be considered for the provisions of this article. Article 111 Counting of period

1. The period of prescription of criminal proceedings shall begin at the moment that the agent consummated the act or, concerning a non-consummated crime, continued crime or habitual crime, on the day that he or she committed the last act of execution. 2. In permanent crimes the period of prescription shall begin at the moment that the consummation ends. 3. Concerning complicity, only the author's act shall be considered. Article 112 Suspension of prescription

1. The prescription of criminal proceedings shall be suspended in cases that the law provides for and also during the time that:
a) According to law, criminal proceedings cannot begin or continue because legal authorization or a sentence that a non-criminal court shall give does not yet exist or because of the devolution of a prejudicial question to a non-criminal judge; b) The delinquent is serving abroad a penalty or security measure that deprives liberty; c) Criminal proceedings are already ongoing, beginning from the moment that the accused receives notification concerning the accusation. 2. The proceedings shall run again at the moment that the cause for suspension ends. 47 (112) Alternative (s) – possibility to choose one thing from two things. Establishes, as an alternative, a prison sentence or a fine penalty = gives the possibility to choose a prison sentence or a fine penalty. 3. The cause for suspension shall not be longer than half of the period provided for in article 110, according to each situation. CHAPTER II
PRESCRIPTION OF PENALTY AND SECURITY MEASURE

Article 113
Period concerning prescription of penalty

1. A penalty shall prescribe in the following periods:
a) 25 years when exceeding 12 years of imprisonment; b) 20 years when exceeding 8 years of imprisonment but not exceeding 12 years; c) 12 years when exceeding 4 years of imprisonment but not exceeding 8 years; d) 8 years when not prison sentences that the previous paragraphs provide for; e) 4 years when the penalty is a fine penalty. 2. The period concerning prescription of penalty shall begin at the moment that the decision that applies the penalty obtains transit in res judicata. Article 114
Prescription of accessory penalty

The prescription of an accessory penalty48 follows the regime of the principal penalty. Article 115
Period of prescription of security measure

Security measures shall prescribe in the following periods:
a) 15 years when depriving liberty; b) 5 years when not depriving liberty; c) 2 years when concerning cancellation of a license to use and possess weapons. 48 (114) Accessory penalty – Penalty that is applied together with the principal penalty to some criminal, such as prohibition to drive a car or possess weapons. Article 116 Suspension of prescription

1. The prescription of penalty and security measure shall be suspended in cases that the law provides for and during the time that:
a) According to law, execution cannot begin or continue; b) Following the convict's escape49 and during the time that he or she has not yet been caught again; c) The convict is serving a penalty or security measure that deprives liberty; d) The period to pay the fine is running; e) The convict cannot temporarily perform community service; f) Execution is already ongoing. 2. Prescription shall run again on the day that the cause for suspension ends. 3. The provisions of article 112 also apply here.

49 (116) Escape (s) - FlightCHAPTER III IMPRESCRIPTIBILITY

Article 117
Crimes of genocide, against peace and humanity and relating to war

Criminal proceedings and penalties for crimes of genocide, against peace and humanity and war crimes do not prescribe. CHAPTER IV
OTHER CAUSES OF EXTINCTION

Article 118 Other causes

Criminal responsibility is extinguished in cases provided by law and by the death of the agent, by amnesty and by pardon. 49 (116) Evasion (s) – By evasion a person flees from prison or the place where he or she is detained. Article 119 Death of the agent

The death of the agent extinguishes criminal proceedings and also the criminal sanction already applied. Article 120 Amnesty

Amnesty extinguishes criminal proceedings and stops the execution of a sanction not yet fully or partially complied with, and also the effects of that sanction and accessory penalties as may be. Article 121
Amnesty and crimes in concurrence

Save for other provisions, amnesty applies to each crime included in the concurrence. Article 122 Pardon

Pardon extinguishes the penalty, totally or partially, or substitutes another penalty provided in law and more favourable to the convicted person in place of that penalty. BOOK II SPECIAL PART

TITLE I
CRIMES AGAINST PEACE AND HUMANITY

CHAPTER I
CRIMES OF GENOCIDE AND AGAINST HUMANITY

Article 123 Genocide

1. A person who, with intent to destroy, totally or partially, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group, commits:
a) Homicide or serious offence against the physical or psychological integrity of that group; b) Acts which, by any means, prevent members of that group from procreating50 or living; c) Rape, sexual slavery, forced prostitution, forced sterilization or other sexual violence of similar gravity; d) Violent separation of members of that group from that group to another group; e) Acts which, by violence, prevent that group from residing or continuing to reside in a geographical space which, according to tradition or history, is considered as belonging to that group; f) Subjection of that group to living conditions or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment, which may one day lead to its total or partial destruction; g) Widespread confiscation or seizure of property belonging to members of that group; h) Prohibition of members of that group from exercising commercial, industrial or professional activities; i) Spreading51 of an epidemic which may cause members of that group to die or suffer offences to their physical integrity; j) Prohibition, omission or impediment, by any means, of members of that group from receiving adequate humanitarian assistance to combat an epidemic or serious food shortage,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. 2. A person who, publicly and directly, incites another to commit genocide shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. 50 (123) By procreation a person has children or generations. 51 (123) Diffusion (s) – By diffusion a person spreads something (in all directions) thereby making that thing live or reach other people or other places. To diffuse = To spread or transmit to others or to other places

Article 124
Crimes against humanity

A person who, in the context of a widespread or systematic attack against any civilian population, commits acts which give rise to:
a) Homicide or serious offence against physical or psychological integrity; b) Extermination, which exists when someone subjects a population totally or partially to adverse living conditions, such as preventing that population from obtaining food or medicine, which may cause one or many persons to die; c) Enslavement; d) Deportation or forced transfer of a population, which exists when someone makes an unlawful displacement of one or many persons to another State or place by expulsion or other coercive acts; e) Imprisonment or other serious means which deprive a person of liberty, contrary to norms or principles of international law; f) Torture, which exists when someone does something to cause serious physical or psychological pain or suffering to a person who has already lost his or her liberty or has already come under the agent's control; g) Rape, sexual slavery, forced prostitution, forced pregnancy, forced sterilization or other sexual violence of similar gravity; h) Persecution, which exists when there is deprivation of the enjoyment of fundamental rights, contrary to international law, of a group or collectivity which can be identified, for political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other motives universally recognized as unacceptable under international law; i) Forced disappearance of persons, which exists when there is detention, imprisonment or kidnapping52

52 (124) Kidnapping (s) – By kidnapping a person seizes and holds another person and does not let him or her leave the place where the agent is holding him or her, against that person's will. which the State or political organization carries out or authorizes, supports or consents to carry out, and, consequently, refuses to recognize that the deprivation of liberty exists or to provide information about that situation or about those persons so that they cannot obtain the protection of the law for a prolonged time; j) Apartheid, which exists when someone commits inhuman acts in the context of an institutionalized regime in which one racial group exercises systematic oppression and domination over one or more other groups, with the intent to maintain53 that regime; k) Other inhuman acts of similar character to those above with intent to cause serious suffering or serious injury or to affect mental or physical health,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. CHAPTER II WAR CRIMES

Article 125 War crimes against persons

1. A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, commits against persons protected by international humanitarian law:
a) Homicide; b) Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment, including biological experiments; c) Serious offence against physical integrity or acts which cause serious suffering; d) Taking of hostages54; e) Compelling service in the enemy armed forces or the recruitment or enlistment of

53 (1249 maintain (v) – to make something or someone continue; to hold something or someone. Police maintain security = Police keep security in place. maintain apartheid = keep apartheid alive. 54 (125) Taking of hostages – Holding a person as a guarantee to demand something from someone. children under 18 years of age into the armed forces, military forces or paramilitary forces of a State, or into armed groups which are not the armed forces, military forces or paramilitary forces of a State, or using them to participate in hostilities55; f) Serious, prolonged and unjustified limitation of a person's liberty; g) Deportation or transfer or unlawful deprivation of liberty; h) Unjustified subtraction or destruction of patrimonial property of great value; i) Conviction and execution of a sentence, when there has not yet been a fair or impartial trial; j) Acts which offend a person's dignity, especially by humiliating and degrading treatment; k) Homicide or wounding of a combatant who has already laid down his or her arms or who, having no more means to defend himself or herself, has already surrendered unconditionally or has by any means already been removed from combat; l) Acts described in the previous article, paragraph g); m) Subjecting a person who has already come into the hands of one belligerent party to physical mutilation or medical or scientific experiments of any type, which are not for medical, dental or hospital treatment and are not done in their interest, which also cause a person to die or create serious danger to a person's health; shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. 2. The penalty shall be aggravated by one fifth in its minimum and maximum limits when the agent commits the acts referred to in the previous number against a member of a humanitarian institution. 3. A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character:
a) Transfers, directly or indirectly, as an occupying power, part of its own civilian population to the occupied territory or transfers the population of the occupied territory, all or part

55 (125) Hostilities (s) - War

only, within that territory or out of that territory; b) Compels prisoners of war or other persons receiving protection to serve in the enemy power's armed forces; c) After the war has ended, delays the repatriation of prisoners of war, without justified motive,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. Article 126
War crimes by prohibited methods

A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or in an armed conflict of non-international character:
a) Attacks the civilian population in general or civilians who do not directly participate in hostilities; b) Attacks civilian property which is not a military objective; c) Attacks, by any means, population centres, dwellings or buildings which are not being defended and are not military objectives; d) Makes an indiscriminate attack which affects the civilian population or property of civilian character, knowing that the attack will cause loss of life, injury to civilians or damage to property of civilian character, which is excessive; e) Takes advantage of the presence of civilians or other protected persons to prevent certain points, zones or military forces from becoming the target of military operations; f) Deliberately causes starvation of the civilian population as a method of warfare, thereby removing from them property they need to live; g) Declares or threatens, in his or her capacity as an official, that no shelter will be given; h) Kills or injures by treachery enemy combatants; i) Makes an attack, even knowing that the attack will cause serious, lasting and grave damage to the environment and, therefore, it can be clearly seen

that the attack is excessive when compared with the concrete and direct overall military advantage foreseen; j) Commits perfidy, understood as acts of killing, injuring or capturing, in which, with intent to deceive, the adversary's good faith is used to make him or her believe that he or she has the right to receive or has the obligation to ensure the protection provided by the rules of international humanitarian law; shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. Article 127
War crimes by prohibited means of warfare

1. A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, uses weapons, projectiles, materials and methods of combat which, according to their nature, cause unnecessary injury or unnecessary suffering or produce indiscriminate effects, contrary to international law applicable to armed conflict, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. 2. The previous number covers, in particular, the use of the following means:
a) Poison or poisoned weapons; b) Asphyxiating, toxic or similar gases, or any analogous liquids, materials or devices; c) Bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets which have a hard casing which does not entirely cover the core or which has incisions; d) Anti-personnel mines; e) Chemical weapons; f) Weapons whose principal effect is to injure by fragments which cannot be located in the human body by X-rays; g) Incendiary weapons; h) Laser56 weapons which cause blindness. 56 (127) Laser (s) – (laser) A device which produces visible light radiation, or with close frequencies, coherent, monochromatic and very strong

3. The weapons, instruments and products referred to in the previous number are those which international law considers as such. Article 128
War crimes against property protected by distinctive insignia or emblem

A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, attacks:
a) Personnel, installations, materials, units or vehicles participating in a mission to maintain peace or provide humanitarian assistance, under the United Nations Charter, when they have the right to the protection which international law gives to persons or civilian property; b) Buildings, installations, materials, units or vehicles, which are marked with the distinctive emblem of the Geneva Conventions or personnel authorized to use those emblems,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 10 to 20 years. Article 129
War crimes against property

A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character:
a) Subtracts, destroys or damages patrimonial property on a large scale or of great value, without military necessity or in an unlawful or arbitrary manner; b) Attacks, destroys or damages buildings dedicated to religious worship, education, art, science or charity, cultural or historical monuments, archaeological sites, hospitals and places where sick and wounded persons are gathered, when they are not military objectives; c) Pillages57 any city or locality, even if taken by assault,

57 (129) Pillage (v) – To loot; to steal, seize or carry away another's property by violence. shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 130
War crimes against other rights

A person who, in the context of an armed conflict of international character or an armed conflict of non-international character, declares abolished, suspended or inadmissible in a tribunal the rights or proceedings of nationals of the enemy party shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. CHAPTER III AGAINST PEACE AND FREEDOM

Article 131 Terrorist organization

1. A group, organization or association of two or more persons who, in order to achieve political, ideological, philosophical or confessional ends, act in concert, with intent to prejudice national integrity or independence, prevent, alter or subvert the functioning of national or international institutions, terrorize or compel public authorities, international organizations or any person, group or the population in general to do something, not do something or tolerate something, by serious crimes:
a) Against the life, physical integrity or liberty of persons; b) Against the security of transport and communications, including telegraphic, telephonic, radio or television communications; c) Which create by intent common danger, by fire, explosion, release of radioactive substances or toxic or asphyxiating gases, flood or avalanche, collapse of construction, contamination of food and drinking water or spreading of disease, harmful pests, plants or animals; d) In which nuclear energy, firearms, explosive substances or devices, incendiary means

of any nature, booby-trapped parcels or letters will be used; e) Acts which destroy or impede means or ways of communication, installations of public services or international installations or installations for supply and satisfaction of the population's vital needs, or divert them from their normal purpose; f) Research and development of biological or chemical weapons. 2. A person who promotes or founds a terrorist group, organization or association, joins, participates in or supports such group, organization or association shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. 3. A person who leads or directs a terrorist group, organization or association shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. 4. When the terrorist group, organization or association or the persons referred to in numbers 2 and 3 possess the means shown in
no. 1, paragraph d), the minimum and maximum limits of the penalty shall be aggravated by one third. 5. Preparatory acts to constitute a terrorist group, organization or association shall give rise to a penalty with its minimum and maximum limits reduced by half. 6. The penalties referred to may receive extraordinary attenuation or not give rise to punishment when the agent voluntarily abandons his or her activity, prevents or makes efforts to prevent the danger which that activity creates or the terrorist group, organization or association from continuing or informs the authorities that the group, organization or association exists, thereby enabling those authorities to prevent them from committing crimes. Article 132 Terrorism

1. A person who commits the crimes provided in the previous article, no. 1, paragraphs a) to c) and e), or any crime by the means referred to in that number's paragraphs d) or f), with the intent referred to therein, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years or the penalty corresponding to the crime committed

with its minimum and maximum limits aggravated by one third, when that penalty is equal or heavier. 2. The penalty may receive special attenuation or not give rise to punishment when the agent voluntarily abandons his or her activity, removes or considerably reduces the danger which that activity creates, prevents the result which the law seeks to avoid from occurring or concretely helps to obtain decisive evidence to identify or capture other responsible persons. Article 133 Financing of terrorism

A person who, by any means, directly or indirectly and intentionally, provides, collects or holds funds or any property and also products or rights which can be converted into funds, seeks to obtain such property, funds, products or rights, with intent to use or knowing that they may be used, totally or partially, to plan, prepare or commit the acts provided in article 131, no. 1, or to commit such acts with the intent referred to in article 132, no. 1, shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. Article 134 Incitement to war

1. A person who, by any means, publicly and repeatedly, incites58 hatred against any race, people or nation, with intent to provoke war or to impede peaceful coexistence between diverse59 races, peoples or nations, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. A person who enlists60 or recruits Timorese or foreign citizens to, by service performed for a foreign group or power, make war against a State or overthrow

58 (134) Incite (v) – To give courage; to incite (someone) to do something; to give stimulus; to enlist. Incite hatred against a race = to stir up hatred against a race. 59 (134) Diverse (adj) – Different; various; which is not the same; which is not only one; which has different aspects; which is not only one kind. 60 (134) Enlist (v) – To incite (someone) to do something; to give courage or stimulus. another state's legitimate government by violent means shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 135 Racial or religious discrimination

1. A person who founds, constitutes an organization or develops organized propaganda to incite or encourage racial or religious discrimination, hatred or violence, and also a person who participates in or provides assistance, including financing, to such organizations or activities shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. 2. A person who, in a public meeting, by writing for dissemination or by any means of social communication, spreads ideas with intent to incite racial or religious discrimination or to encourage or provoke violent acts against any person or group because of his or her race, colour, ethnic origin or religion, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. CHAPTER IV COMMON PROVISIONS

Article 136
Responsibility of military commander or other superior

1. A military commander or one who acts as a military commander who, having known that forces under his or her command and effective control or under his or her responsibility and effective control are committing or are about to commit any crime provided in this title, does not adopt necessary and adequate measures to prevent or repress that practice or to immediately inform the competent authorities shall be punished with the penalty corresponding to that crime or to the crime which will be committed. 2. The provisions of the previous number shall also apply, with necessary adaptations, to a superior regarding control of subordinates who are under his or her authority and effective control. Article 137

Definitions
For the purposes of this title, the following shall be considered:
a) "Armed conflict of international character" one which:
i) Occurs between states, even without a formal declaration of war, even if one of those States does not recognize that state of war; ii) Corresponds to total or partial occupation of the territory of a State, even if that occupation does not meet military resistance; iii) Corresponds to a situation in which peoples fight against colonial domination, foreign occupation and regimes which practice segregation, when the people exercise their right to self-determination enshrined in the United Nations Charter and in the declaration on principles of international law concerning friendly relations and cooperation between States; b) "Armed conflict of non-international character" one which occurs in the territory of a State, is of prolonged character and pits governmental authorities against organized groups or organized groups against each other, with the exception of situations in which there are disturbances and in which there are internal tensions, such as sporadic or isolated acts of violence or others of similar character; c) "Protected person":
i) In international armed conflict, persons protected for the purposes of the Geneva Conventions of 1949 and Additional Protocol I, namely wounded, sick, shipwrecked, prisoners of war, sanitary or religious personnel and the civilian population; ii) In armed conflict of non-international character, wounded, sick and shipwrecked persons, and also persons who do not take active part in hostilities but have come into the enemy's hands; iii) In armed conflict of international character and in armed conflict of national character, members of the armed forces and combatants of the enemy party who have already laid down their arms or have no other means of defence. TITLE II CRIMES AGAINST PERSONS

CHAPTER I AGAINST LIFE

Article 138 Simple homicide

A person who kills another person shall be punished with imprisonment from 8 to 20 years. Article 139 Aggravated homicide

An agent who kills another person in circumstances which show special censurability or perversity, namely, because:
a) Using poison, torture, asphyxiation, fire, explosives or other insidious means or means which constitute the commission of a crime of common danger, or other cruel acts, to increase the victim's suffering; b) Using dissimulation or other means or resources to make it difficult for the victim to defend himself or herself or make it impossible for the victim to defend himself or herself; c) Killing for greed61, for pleasure in killing, for seeking sexual excitement or satisfaction, for payment or reward or promise of payment or reward or for other futile or base motive; d) Killing to prepare, execute or conceal another crime, facilitate escape or ensure impunity for the agent of a crime; e) Killing because of racial, religious or political hatred; 61 (139) Greed – Strong and insatiable desire; greedy (adj); covetous (for something)

f) Killing with premeditation, which exists when he or she uses coldness62 of mind, reflection on the means used or maintains his or her intent to kill for 24 hours; g) When the victim is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, collateral or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person who lives with him or her in a relationship in which there is hierarchical, economic or labour dependence; h) The victim is very vulnerable because of his or her age, illness or physical or mental disability; i) The victim is a member of a sovereign organ or constitutional political organ, member of an organ of administration, magistrate, defender, lawyer, officer of justice, official or other person who holds public service, namely while he or she is exercising his or her function or because of the function he or she holds; j) The victim is a witness, declarant, expert, injured party or offended person and the agent commits the crime to prevent testimony, denunciation of facts or complaint or because of his or her intervention in proceedings,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. Article 140 Negligent homicide

1. A person who, by negligence, kills another person shall be punished with imprisonment up to 4 years or a fine. 2. In cases where the agent acts with gross negligence63 he or she shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years. Article 141* Interruption of pregnancy

62 (139) Coldness of mind – cold heart
63 (140) Gross (adj) – Gross (adj) – Heavy; serious. Gross negligence = serious negligence; serious lack of care. A person acts with gross negligence when he or she lacks excessive care when doing that thing. 1. A person who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and without that woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. A person who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and with that woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years. 3. A pregnant woman who gives consent to another person to perform an abortion, or, herself or through another person, provokes an abortion on herself shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years. 4. The provisions of the previous number do not apply when, according to existing medical knowledge and experience and having done everything possible to save the life of the pregnant woman and the fetus or embryo, interruption of pregnancy is the only way to remove death from the pregnant woman, namely with medical certification and performed by a doctor or other health professional, under the direction of a doctor, in a public health establishment or officially recognized, and with the pregnant woman's consent. 5. Before performing the interruption a panel of three doctors must certify the circumstances referred to in the previous number, in a written and signed medical certificate; in that panel there shall be one doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the interruption of pregnancy. 6. The consent of the pregnant woman shall be given by herself in a written and signed document, having heard, when possible, her spouse or the person who lives with her other than her husband, or another person, according to that woman's request, who gives it, and, when possible, shall be given two days before performing that interruption. 7. When the pregnant woman is a minor, her legal representative shall give consent. 8. When the pregnant woman is an adult or emancipated person who is psychologically incapable or a person who is suffering from psychological incapacity, her spouse or the person who lives with her other than her husband, or her legal representative, ascendant or descendant, or, if there is none, any collateral relative, respectively and successively, shall give that consent. 9. When it is not possible to obtain the medical certificate referred to in no. 5 and/or it is not possible to obtain consent as written in
nos. 6 to 8, and the interruption must be performed quickly and cannot wait any longer, the doctor, looking at each situation, shall decide, also using, when possible, the opinion of other doctors. 10. In the panel referred to in no. 5 there shall be a doctor who has adequate knowledge to evaluate the circumstances which justify the interruption of pregnancy, and, when possible, one gynecologist/obstetrician. 11. The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the interruption of pregnancy must provide necessary clarifications to the pregnant woman and, when applicable, to the persons referred to in nos. 7 and 8, namely about the method to be used in the interruption, the effects of that interruption itself and its consequences for the pregnant woman's physical and psychological health. 12. Doctors and other health professionals have the right to present a conscientious objection to any act relating to the interruption of pregnancy. 13. Doctors and other health professionals who invoke a conscientious objection must guarantee the immediate intervention of other health professionals in the acts which must be performed and also immediate accompaniment of the pregnant woman. 14. Conscientious objection must be expressed explicitly and must be notified to the clinical head of the health establishment in which the objector works. * Law 6/2009, of 15 July, introduced this wording. The previous wording of Article 141 is as follows:
Article 141 Interruption of pregnancy
1. A person who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and without that woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. A person who causes a pregnant woman to abort, by any means and with that woman's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years. 3. A pregnant woman who gives consent to another person to perform an abortion, or, herself or through another person, provokes an abortion on herself shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years. 4. The provisions of the previous numbers do not apply in cases where the interruption of pregnancy constitutes the only means to avoid danger of death to the pregnant woman or to the fetus or serious and irreversible injury to her body or to her physical or psychological health, namely performed by a doctor or health professional, in a health establishment, with the authorization and supervision of a medical board and with the consent of the pregnant woman or her spouse. 5. Specific legislation shall regulate the provisions of no. 4

Article 142 Infanticide

A mother who kills her child during childbirth or immediately after childbirth and when she is still under the disturbing influence of childbirth shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. Article 143 Exposure or abandonment
1. One who intentionally creates danger to another person's life:
a) By placing that person in a place where he or she must face a situation in which he or she cannot defend himself or herself alone; or
b) By abandoning that person when he or she cannot defend himself or herself because of his or her age, physical disability or illness, and the agent has an obligation to look after, care for or provide assistance to that person,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 6 years. 2. When that fact:
a) Causes serious offence to the victim's physical integrity, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years; b) Causes the victim to die, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. 3. When that victim is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, collateral, affine up to the second degree, adopter or adoptee, or person who lives with him or her as husband and wife, the minimum and maximum limits of the penalty shown in the previous numbers shall increase by one third. Article 144 Incitement or assistance to suicide

1. One who incites another person to kill himself or herself or provides assistance to another person to kill himself or herself, that suicide occurring either in attempted or consummated form, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent commits the facts described in the previous number against any person shown in the previous article, number 3, or against a minor under 17 years of age or against a person who has significantly diminished capacity to make valuations or determinations, the penalty shall increase to imprisonment up to 5 years. 3. A person who, by any means and repeatedly, makes public propaganda for suicide shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. CHAPTER II
AGAINST PHYSICAL INTEGRITY
Article 145
Simple offence against physical integrity

1. One who offends another person's body or health shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. To conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 146
Serious offence against physical integrity

One who offends another person's body or health with intent to:
a) Cause that person to lose any organ or limb; b) Seriously and permanently disfigure that person; c) Seriously and permanently or definitively affect that person's capacity to work, his or her intellectual capacity or his or her capacity to have children; d) Provoke permanent illness or incurable psychological anomaly; or

e) Create danger to his or her life,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. Article 147 Aggravation

1. One who, wanting only to offend another person's body or health:
a) Shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years, when he or she causes the offence provided in article 146; b) Shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 6 years, when he or she causes that person to die by negligence. 2. One who, wanting only to cause the offence provided in article 146, but causes a person to die by negligence, shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. 3. When the agent commits the crimes referred to in the two previous articles against any person contemplated in article 139, paragraph i), because that victim holds the function written therein or when that victim is exercising his or her function, the penalty shall be aggravated by one third in its minimum and maximum limits, when other legal provisions do not provide a heavier penalty. Article 148
Negligent offence against physical integrity

1. One who offends another person's body or health by negligence shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. 2. If that negligence is gross, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 3. When that fact causes serious bodily offence the agent shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 4.In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint.
Article 149
Medical-surgical intervention and treatment

1. Intervention and treatment which, according to the experience and knowledge obtained in medicine, is indicated and performed by a doctor or person with legal authorization, as "leges artis", to prevent, diagnose, control or reduce illness, injury, bodily lesion or fatigue or mental disturbance shall not be considered as bodily offence. 2. Where, due to violation of "leges artis", danger arises to the patient's body, health or life, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 3. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 150
Bodily offence with poisonous substance

1. Whoever offends another person's body or health by administering to them a poisonous or harmful substance to physical or mental health shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 5 years. 2. Where the act produces any consequence provided for in article 146 or causes the victim's death, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years or from 4 to 12 years, respectively. Article 151
Reciprocal bodily offence

1. Where two persons mutually offend each other's body or health but they do not commit the act in self-defence and there is no circumstance provided for in article 146 and none of the participants dies, they shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 152 Participation in affray

1. Whoever intervenes or participates with two or more persons in an affray, which causes a person's death or produces serious bodily offence, but these consequences cannot be imputed to them intentionally, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Participation in an affray does not give rise to punishment when it occurs due to a non-censurable motive, namely because the agent participates to react to an attack, to defend another person or to separate the contenders. Article 153
Ill-treatment of incapacitated person

1. Whoever is responsible for caring for or educating or has as a subordinate in work activity an incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, advanced age, pregnancy, physical or mental disability, and causes offence to that person's body or health, or subjects them to physical or mental ill-treatment, or cruel treatment shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years, where another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty. 2. Where the victim is the agent's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee, or person living with them as spouse, the penalty referred to in the previous number shall be increased by one third in its minimum and maximum limits. Article 154
Ill-treatment of spouse

A person who commits physical or mental ill-treatment of their spouse or of a person living with them as spouse shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years, where another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty. Article 155 Ill-treatment of minor

1. A person who must care for a minor under 17 years of age, has responsibility for their education or uses them as a subordinate in work activity and:
a) Causes offence to their body or health, commits physical and mental ill-treatment or cruel treatment of them; b) Subjects them to economic exploitation, to dangerous work or work which may compromise their education or their physical, mental, spiritual, moral or social development; c) Subjects them to any form of slavery or analogous practice; d) Uses, recruits or offers them for use in prostitution, to produce pornographic material or to perform in pornographic shows; or
e) Uses, recruits or offers them for use to commit illicit acts or activities, namely in the production or trafficking of narcotics as defined by international convention,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years, where another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty. 2. Whoever, in such a situation, uses a minor to beg as a poor person, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years, where another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty. 3. Where the victim is the agent's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person living with them in similar condition, the penalties referred to in the previous numbers shall be increased by one third in their minimum and maximum limits. Article 156 Aggravation by result

Where the ill-treatment described in articles 153 to 155 produces the effects described in article 146 the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years, and, where it causes death, imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. CHAPTER III
CRIMES AGAINST PERSONAL LIBERTY

SECTION I PERSONAL ASSAULT

Article 157 Threat

1. Whoever, by any means, threatens another person with a crime so as to cause them fear or unease or so as to prejudice their freedom to determine themselves shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 158 Coercion

1. Whoever, by violence or threat of significant harm, compels someone to do something or not do something or tolerate some activity shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 159 Serious coercion

Where the agent commits coercion:
a) With threat of a crime punishable with imprisonment exceeding 3 years; b) By seriously abusing a function which they, as an official, have; c) Against a person who cannot defend themselves, due to their age, disability, illness or pregnancy; d) Against a person referred to in article 139, paragraph i), they shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. Article 160 Kidnapping

1. Whoever, in a situation not covered by the provisions of criminal procedural law, detains, imprisons, maintains in prison or detention another person or, in any way, deprives a person of liberty shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Imprisonment from 2 to 8 years shall be applied where:
a) The deprivation of liberty lasts more than 72 hours; b) The agent commits the deprivation with offence to physical integrity, torture or other cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment; c) The deprivation causes the victim's death, due to the agent's negligence, or causes the victim to commit suicide; d) The agent commits the deprivation against a person who exercises public, religious or political authority; e) An agent of authority or member of a political organization promotes, authorizes or supports the deprivation. Article 161 Abduction

1. Whoever, by violence, threat or trickery, transfers another person from one place to another with the intention to:
a) Subject the victim to extortion; b) Commit a crime of assault, exploitation or sexual abuse; c) Obtain ransom or reward; or
d) Compel public authority or another person to commit an act or omission or endure some activity,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. 2. Imprisonment from 5 to 15 years shall be applied where there also exist circumstances provided for in article 160, number 2. Article 162 Enslavement

1. Whoever, by any means, places another person as a slave and uses them in that condition, shall be punished with imprisonment from 8 to 20 years. 2. The victim's consent has no relevance, where the agent uses any means referred to in the following article. 3. For the purposes of applying the provisions of this article, a person shall be considered in a situation of slavery who is subject to submission, even if only de facto, to power corresponding to a right of ownership, or other real right, or linked to the destination of a thing. Article 163 Trafficking in persons

1. A person who recruits, alienates, cedes, acquires, transports, transfers, lodges or receives someone, by threat, force or other coercion, by abduction, by fraud, by deception, by abusing their authority or abusing that person's vulnerable situation, or by delivering or receiving payment or some benefit to obtain consent from a person who has authority over another person, for the purpose of obtaining exploitation, shall be punished with imprisonment from 8 to 20 years. 2. A person who recruits, transports, transfers, lodges or receives a person under 17 years of age for the purpose of exploitation shall be punished with the penalty provided for in the previous number, even if it does not involve the means referred to in the previous number. 3. For the purposes of applying the provisions of this article, exploitation must include, at least, exploitation of another person's prostitution or other sexual exploitation, forced labour or service, slavery or practices similar to slavery, or servitude or extraction of organs. 4. The victim's consent has no relevance, where the agent uses any means referred to in no. 1. Article 164 Aggravation

Where the agent commits the acts described in articles 162 and 163
a) To facilitate their own or another person's sexual exploitation or use of the victim; b) Against a victim who is not yet 17 years old; c) Against a victim who is in a foreign country or whom they have taken to a foreign country; d) Uses the victim, against their will, to commit a crime; or
e) At a time when they exercise an activity which gives them public or religious authority in a group, region or in the whole country,
they shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. Article 165 Trafficking in human organs

1. A person who obtains, alienates, cedes, acquires, transports or transfers another person's tissue, organ, substance or body part, without consent or, for that purpose, uses threat, force or other coercion, abduction, fraud, deception, abuse of their authority or of the victim's vulnerable situation, or by delivering or receiving payment or some benefit, or assists another person to obtain, transact, transport or store such tissue, organ, substance or body, shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. 2. Where the acts referred to in the previous number produce any consequence provided for in article 146 or cause the victim's death, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 20 years and from 5 to 20 years, respectively. 3. The victim's consent has no criminal relevance, where the agent uses any means referred to in no. 1. Article 166 Sale of persons

1. Whoever, in a situation not covered by the provisions of article 136, by any act or transaction, transfers a person or group of persons to another person or group of persons in order to receive as payment some amount or consideration, reward or other advantage shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. Where the agent commits the acts referred to in the previous number:
a) Against a person who is not yet 17 years old; b) By abusing authority arising from family, tutelary or curatelary relationship, economic or work dependency; c) By taking advantage of a function or place which, by any title, they exercise in a prison establishment, establishment for education or correction, hospital, hospice, asylum, clinic or other health establishment or establishment for assistance or treatment; or
d) Against an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability,
they shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 12 years. 3. The victim's consent or the consent of a person who exercises some power over the victim has no criminal relevance. Article 167
Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment

1. Whoever, with a function to prevent, investigate, decide on any typical offence, execute the respective sanction or protect, guard, watch or accompany a detained or imprisoned person, commits torture or cruel degrading or inhuman treatment of that person, in order to:
a) Obtain from that person or from another person a confession, testimony, statement or information; b) Punish that person for an act which that person or another person has already committed or which they think that person or another person has committed; c) Frighten that person or another person, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. A person who, on their own initiative, on superior order or by agreement with the competent entity to exercise the functions referred to in the previous number, de facto performs those functions by committing any act described therein shall also be punished with the penalty provided for in the previous number. 3. Torture or cruel, degrading or inhuman treatment shall be considered to exist where any act causes acute physical or psychological suffering, serious physical or psychological exhaustion or there is any act using chemical products, drugs or other means, natural or artificial, with the intention to disturb the victim's capacity to make decisions or show their will freely. Article 168 Aggravation

1. A person who, as a rule and in the conditions described in the previous article:
a) Causes serious bodily offence, as described in article 146; b) Uses particularly serious means or methods of torture, such as beating, electric shock, execution simulation, hallucinogenic substances, sexual abuse or serious threat to family members; c) Commits those acts to prevent or make it difficult for someone to freely exercise their political or associative rights enshrined in the Constitution; d) Habitually commits the acts described in this article,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. 2. Where the acts described in this article or in the previous article cause the victim to commit suicide or die the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 20 years. Article 169 Omission of denunciation

1. A hierarchical superior who, having learned that their subordinate has committed any act described in articles 167 and 168, does not make a denunciation within 3 days starting from the day they obtained that knowledge, shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 6 years. 2. Whoever, having obtained knowledge due to their profession and officially that someone has committed any act described in articles 167 and 168, does not immediately communicate to their hierarchical superior or does not make the respective denunciation shall be punished with the penalty fixed in the previous number extraordinarily mitigated. Article 170
Freedom of assembly and demonstration

Whoever interferes in an assembly or demonstration in a public place or open to the public, legally authorized, such as preventing or seeking to prevent the assembly or demonstration from taking place, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. An agent of police authority who prevents or seeks to prevent, in a situation not covered by legal conditionality, someone from exercising their right to assembly or demonstration described in the previous number shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years.

SECTION II SEXUAL ASSAULT

Article 171 Sexual coercion

Whoever compels another person to suffer or commit, with them or with another person, a relevant sexual act, by violence, serious threat, or, for that purpose, first causes that person to lose consciousness or be unable to resist, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. Article 172 Rape

Whoever, by the means referred to in the previous article, has with another person vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus or compels that person to allow the insertion of any object into their anus or vagina shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 173 Aggravation

An agent who commits the sexual assaults referred to in articles 171 and 172:
a) By abusing authority arising from family, tutelary or curatelary relationship, hierarchical, economic or work dependency; b) By taking advantage of a function or place which, by any title, they exercise in a prison establishment, establishment for education or correction, hospital, hospice, asylum, clinic or other health establishment or other establishment for assistance or treatment; c) Against an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability; d) Against a victim under 17 years old,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years, in the case provided for in article 171, and imprisonment from 5 to 20 years, in the case provided for in article 172. SECTION III SEXUAL EXPLOITATION

Article 174 Sexual exploitation of another person

1. Whoever, with the intention to obtain profit or uses the activity as their daily work, facilitates or, in any way, contributes to another person exercising prostitution or committing other sexual acts shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. 2. The agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years, where there also exist any of the following circumstances:
a) Exploitation of a victim who is in abandonment or economic need; b) Violence, serious threat or coercion of the victim; c) The victim's displacement from the country where they live or reside to another country; d) Retention of any of the victim's identification documents. Article 175 Child prostitution

1. Whoever commits acts of sexual exploitation referred to in the previous article against a person who is not yet 17 years old, even with the victim's consent, shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years, in the case provided for in the previous article's number 1, and with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years, in the case where there exists any circumstance covered by the previous article's number 2. 2. Whoever offers, obtains, seeks or delivers a person who is not yet 17 years old for child prostitution shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years, where another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty. Article 176 Child pornography

1. A person who, for predominantly sexual purposes, exposes or represents a person who is not yet 17 years old performing sexual activity, real or simulated, or, by any means, exhibits such activity or the minor's sexual organs shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. 2. This penalty shall also be applied to a person who produces, shares, disseminates, imports, exports, sells, possesses means of communication, instruments, documents or records for the purpose referred to in the previous number or to divulge those acts. SECTION IV SEXUAL ABUSE

Article 177 Sexual abuse of minor

1. Whoever commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus with a child who is not yet 14 years old shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 20 years. 2. Whoever commits a relevant sexual act with a child who is not yet 14 years old shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 178
Sexual acts with adolescent

An adult who, in a situation not covered by the provisions of this section, commits a relevant sexual act with a child aged 14 to 16, by abusing their inexperience shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 5 years. Article 179
Sexual abuse of person unable to resist

Whoever commits a relevant sexual act with an unconscious or incapacitated person, particularly vulnerable due to illness, physical or mental disability, by taking advantage of their incapacity, shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. Article 180 Sexual fraud

1. A person who, by taking advantage of a person's error regarding their identity, commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus or a relevant sexual act with that person, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 181 Sexual exhibitionism

1. A person who publicly disturbs another person by committing acts of a sexual character shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A person who commits vaginal coitus, anal coitus or oral coitus in the presence of another person, against that person's will, even if in private, shall also be punished with this penalty. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 4. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. SECTION V COMMON PROVISIONS

Article 182 Aggravation

1. The penalties provided for in section II to section IV of this chapter shall be aggravated by one third in their minimum and maximum limits where:
a) The victim's age is less than 12 years at the time the agent commits the act; b) The agent transmits to the victim a venereal, syphilitic disease or acquired immunodeficiency syndrome; c) Due to the act the victim dies, commits suicide or attempts suicide; d) The victim is the agent's descendant, collateral, relative or affine up to the second degree, adoptee or person living with them in similar condition or in a relationship giving rise to hierarchical, economic or work dependency. 2. Where several circumstances provided for in the previous number concur at once, only one of those circumstances shall be relevant as a circumstance which modifies the legal type's abstract framework; the others enter into the assessment to determine the concrete penalty. CHAPTER V AGAINST PRIVATE LIFE
Article 183 Disclosure

1. A person who, by even lawful means, learns facts about another person's private or sexual life and, without that person's consent and without just cause, discloses those facts publicly shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 184 Violation of secrecy

1. A person who, without consent, reveals another person's secret which they learned due to their status, office, employment, profession or art shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 2. Where the secret concerns commercial, industrial, professional or artistic activity, which they learned in the circumstances described in the previous number, and causes prejudice to another person or to the State, imprisonment of 2 years or a fine shall be applied. 3. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 185 Violation of domicile

1. A person who enters another person's dwelling without consent or, having authorization to enter, remains in the dwelling after receiving notification to leave, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. Where, to make the crime easier, they use nighttime, take advantage of a dwelling located in a deserted place, gather with other persons so that together they are three or more persons, use a weapon, use violence, threaten with violence or use climbing, breaking in or false key, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 3. Where at the time the agent commits the crime someone is inside the house, the penalty provided for in the previous number's minimum and maximum limits shall be increased by one third. 4. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 5. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 186
Entry into other place closed to the public

1. A person who commits the act described in the previous article, numbers 1 and 2, in another place which is closed or fenced with a wall and to which the public cannot freely enter shall be punished with the penalties provided for in those numbers, respectively, with their minimum and maximum limits reduced by half. 2. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 187
Violation of correspondence or telecommunication

1. A person who, without consent and in a case which procedural rules do not admit, opens a package, letter or other writing intended for another person or obtains knowledge of their content or does not allow the addressee to receive the package, letter or other writing shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. This penalty shall also be applied to a person who, in those circumstances, interferes with or learns the content of telephone, telegraph or other telecommunication communication. 3. A person who discloses the content of a letter, package, closed writing, telephone call or other communication referred to in the previous number, even if they learned the content by lawful means, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 4. Where an official in the post, telegraph, telephone or telecommunications commits the acts described in the previous numbers the penalty's minimum and maximum limits shall be aggravated by one third. 5. In order to conduct criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. TITLE III
CRIMES AGAINST LIFE IN DEMOCRACY

CHAPTER I
CRIMES AGAINST PEACE AND PUBLIC TRANQUILITY

Article 188 Criminal association

1. A person who promotes or establishes a group, organization or association which uses as its purpose or its activity the commission of crimes shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. A group, organization or criminal association shall be considered a gathering of two or more persons who, over a period of time, work together to commit or cause another person to commit crimes, in order to disturb public order or to obtain directly or indirectly some benefit or advantage. 3. A person who joins a criminal group, organization or association, supports a criminal group, organization or association, or participates in the activity of a criminal group, organization or association shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 4. A person who leads or directs the groups, organizations or associations referred to in the previous numbers shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. 5. The penalties feared in the previous numbers may receive extraordinary mitigation where the agent prevents or strives strongly to prevent the group, organization or association from continuing or informs the authority of the existence of those groups in order to enable the authority to prevent those groups from committing crimes. Article 189 Incitement to crime

1. A person who, publicly and by any means, incites persons to commit any crime shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A person who, in private or in public, praises or gives reward to a person who commits any crime in order to incite other persons to commit similar crimes shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 3. Where in the cases feared in the previous numbers someone actually commits the crime which the agent wanted to incite persons to commit, that agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 5 years, where another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty. Article 190 Participation in riot

1. Whoever participates in a public riot, in which persons collectively commit violence against persons or property, shall be punished with imprisonment of 1 year or a fine, where another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty. 2. Where the agent provokes or directs the riot, they shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years. 3. If the riot is armed, the penalty referred to in the previous numbers shall be doubled in its minimum and maximum limits. Article 191
Impediment to exercise of political right

A person who, by violence, prevents another person from exercising their political right shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. Article 192 Influence peddling

1. A person who, themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another person, a patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or promise of such advantage, for them to abuse influence, real or supposed, which they exercise over any public entity, shall be punished with:
a) Imprisonment from 2 to 6 years, namely where another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty, where they have the objective to obtain some favorable unlawful decision; b) Imprisonment of 1 year or a fine, namely where another legal provision does not establish a heavier penalty, where they have the objective to obtain some favorable lawful decision. 2. A person who, themselves or using another person, with their authorization or ratification, gives or promises a patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage to the persons referred to in the previous number to obtain the purpose referred to in the previous number, paragraph a), shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 4 years or a fine. Article 193 Disobedience to order to disperse

1. A person who does not follow a legitimate order to leave a public gathering or assembly, which legitimate authority gives, with warning that, if they do not follow it, they commit a crime, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. If the disobedient person provokes the gathering or assembly, they shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. Article 194
Abuse of public signal or uniform

1. A person who abusively uses a signal or call for alarm or help, or pretends to make persons believe that they need another person's help due to disaster, danger or situation of collective need shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 2.The same penalty shall also be imposed on anyone who improperly or abusively uses a uniform, attire, costume or insignia that identifies a public or international activity, authority or institution as a means to facilitate the commission of some illicit act. Article 195 Usurpation of function

1. Anyone who, without authorization, exercises a function or practices an act that only a public official, military command or public security force may practice, thereby presenting themselves, expressly or tacitly, as a person having such capacity, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2.
